<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
There exists a $\mathbb{C}$-linear exact equivalence of abelian categories 
  \[\mathsf{SM}: \mathsf{Conn}_{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X}, D) \to \mathsf{Higgs}_{\mathrm{nilp}}^{0, \mathrm{ss}}(\bar{X}, D)\]
  extending the Simpson-Mochizuki equivalence $\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}$ on polystable bundles.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
The curve assumption is mainly used for the comparison \cref{thm:relative-DR-Dol-quasi-isom-with-coeff}. If this can be generalized to higher dimensions, perhaps using a multi-V-filtration, one should get the full correspondence in all dimensions.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
A logarithmic connection $(E, \nabla)$ is a vector bundle $E$ on $\bar X$ with a $\mathbb{C}$-linear map $\nabla: E \to E \otimes \Omega_{\bar X}^1(\log D)$ satisfying the product rule $\nabla(fs) = \mathrm{d} f \otimes s + f\nabla s$ for local sections $f \in \mathcal{O}_{\bar X}$ and $s \in E$. Locally, using a holomorphic frame around $p \in \bar{X}$, one can write 
  \[\nabla = \mathrm{d} + A(z)\frac{\mathrm{d} z}{z}\]
  and the residue is defined to be 
  \[\mathrm{Res}_p\nabla \coloneq A(0) \in \mathrm{End}(E_p)\]
  which is independent, up to conjugacy, of the choice of coordinate and frame.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
A logarithmic Higgs bundle $(E, \theta)$ is a vector bundle $E$ on $\bar{X}$ with a $\mathcal{O}_{\bar X}$-linear map $\theta: E \to E \otimes \Omega_{\bar X}^1(\log D)$. Locally, $\theta$ is a matrix of logarithmic one-forms, and its residue $\mathrm{Res}_p\theta$ is defined to be the coefficient of $\mathrm{d} z/ z$, valued at $p$. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} We say that $E$ is stable (resp. semistable) if for every nonzero proper subbundle $F \subset E$, with $\theta(F) \subset F \otimes \Omega_{\bar X}^1(\log D)$, we have $\mu(F) < \mu(E)$ (resp. $\mu(F) \leq \mu(E)$) where $\mu = \deg / \operatorname{rank}$.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
A complex mixed twistor structure (MTS) is a pair $(V, W_\bullet)$, where $V$ is a vector bundle on $\mathbb{P}^1$, and $W_\bullet V$ is a locally split increasing filtration, called the weight filtration, by subbundles such that 
  \[\mathrm{gr}^W_k V \simeq \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(k)^{\oplus m_k} \hspace*{\parindent} k \in \mathbb{Z}\]
  We say that $(V, W_\bullet)$ is pure of weight $k$ if $V \simeq \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(k)^{\oplus m}$. A morphism of MTS is an $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}$-linear filtered map.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:delignesplitting}
  Fix a point $\lambda \in \mathbb{P}^1$. Every mixed twistor structure admits a functorial splitting of its weight filtration when restricted to $\mathbb{P}^1 \backslash \set{\lambda}$. Furthermore, the splitting is compatible with tensor products and duals.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
A $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra is a commutative unital algebra object $\mathcal{A}$ in $\mathsf{MTS}_\mathbb{C}$, i.e., $\mathcal{A} \in \mathsf{MTS}_\mathbb{C}$ with morphisms of MTS
  \[\mathcal{T}(0) \xrightarrow{u} \mathcal{A}, \hspace*{\parindent} \mathcal{A} \otimes \mathcal{A} \xrightarrow{\mu_{\mathcal{A}}} \mathcal{A}\]
  satisfying the usual unit, associativity, and commutativity diagrams. Similarly, we can define a $\bm{\Lambda}-$MTS-algebra (and module) from a $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra $\bm{\Lambda}$. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Now, $\mathcal{A}$ is also a coherent commutative $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}-$algebra, so we can view it as a relative affine scheme $\operatorname{Spec}_{\mathbb{P}^1} \mathcal{A} \to \mathbb{P}^1$. Then it makes sense to define $\abs{\mathcal{A}} \coloneq \mathcal{A}\vert_{\lambda = 1}$, which is a complex algebra.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
An artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra is a $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra $\mathcal{A}$ for which $\abs{\mathcal{A}}$ is a local artinian $\mathbb{C}$-algebra with residue field $\mathbb{C}$, together with a morphism $\mathcal{A} \twoheadrightarrow \mathcal{T}(0)$. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Since $\mathsf{MTS}_\mathbb{C}$ is abelian, $\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{A}} \coloneq \ker(\mathcal{A} \twoheadrightarrow \mathcal{T}(0))$ is a locally free sheaf on $\mathbb{P}^1$. We have a splitting coming from the unit morphism $\mathcal{T}(0) \to \mathcal{A}$, hence a decomposition $\mathcal{A} = \mathcal{T}(0) \oplus \mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{A}}$. Morphisms of artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebras preserve the unit and residue morphisms, and together these form a category $\mathsf{MTS\text{-}Art}$.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:affinesplitting}
  Let $\mathcal{A}$ be an artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra and $\mathcal{K} = \brac*{\mathcal{K}^\bullet, \mathrm{d}^\bullet}$ a bounded complex of $\mathcal{A}$-MTS-modules. On $\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda \coloneq \mathbb{P}^1 \setminus \set{\infty}$ we have a functorial isomorphism of complexes of $\abs*{\mathcal{A}} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}$-modules
  \[\varphi_{\mathcal{K}}: \mathcal{K}\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \simeq \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \abs*{\mathcal{K}}\]
  which is compatible with morphisms, derived tensor products, and artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra coefficient base change.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
The functorial splitting of \cref{lem:delignesplitting} gives 
  \[\phi_{\mathcal{A}}:  \mathcal{A}\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \brac*{\mathrm{gr}^W \mathcal{A}}\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Since $\mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathcal{A}$ is pure of weight $k$, evaluation gives an isomorphism 
  \[\operatorname{ev}_k: H^0\brac*{\mathbb{P}^1, \mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathcal{A} \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(-k)} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(k) \xrightarrow{\simeq} \mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathcal{A}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Now, notice that we also have an isomorphism coming from evaluation at $\lambda = 1$:
  \[H^0\brac*{\mathbb{P}^1, \mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathcal{A} \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(-k)} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathcal{A}\vert_{\lambda = 1} = \abs*{\mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathcal{A}}\]
  thus we can trivialize $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(k)\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}$ to get $\tau_{\mathcal{A}}: \mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathcal{A}\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \abs{\mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathcal{A}}$ which is also functorial (since evaluations are functorial) and just the identity at $\lambda = 1$. Collecting these isomorphisms, we end up with 
  \[\varphi_{\mathcal{A}}\coloneq (1 \otimes \phi_{\mathcal{A},1}^{-1}) \circ \tau_{\mathcal{A}} \circ \phi_{\mathcal{A}}: \mathcal{A}\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \abs{\mathcal{A}} \] 
  which is functorial, and compatible with tensor products. Then functoriality with respect to the multiplication $\mathcal{A} \otimes \mathcal{A} \to \mathcal{A}$ and unit $\mathcal{T}(0) \to \mathcal{A}$ implies that $\varphi_{\mathcal{A}}$ is an isomorphism of algebras. Let $\mathcal{M}$ be a $\mathcal{A}-$MTS-module, then we have the same isomorphism $\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}$, and functoriality with respect to the module map $\mathcal{A} \otimes \mathcal{M} \to \mathcal{M}$ gives that $\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}$ is an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \abs*{\mathcal{A}}$-modules. \\
  \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Moving on to a complex $\mathcal{K} = \brac*{\mathcal{K}^\bullet, \mathrm{d}^\bullet}$, for each $i$ we have 
  \[\varphi_{\mathcal{K}^i}: \mathcal{K}^i\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes \abs{\mathcal{K}^i}\]
  and since each $\mathrm{d}^i$ is a morphism of $\mathcal{A}-$MTS-modules, functoriality assembles these $\varphi_{\mathcal{K}^i}$ into a isomorphism $\varphi_{\mathcal{K}}$of complexes. Clearly $\varphi_{\mathcal{K}}$ is functorial. Now suppose we have another complex $\mathcal{M} = \brac*{\mathcal{M}^\bullet, \mathrm{d}^\bullet}$, then individual tensor compatibility and flatness of $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}$ over $\mathbb{C}$ implies 
  \begin{align*}
    \brac*{\mathcal{K} \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_\mathcal{A} \mathcal{M}} \vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} &\simeq \mathcal{K}\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathcal{A}\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}} \mathcal{M}\vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} &\text{since open restriction is exact}\\
    &\simeq \brac*{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \abs{\mathcal{K}}} \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \abs{\mathcal{A}}} \brac*{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \abs{\mathcal{M}}}\\
    &\simeq \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \brac*{\abs{\mathcal{K}} \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\abs{\mathcal{A}}} \abs{\mathcal{M}}} &\text{since $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \abs{\mathcal{A}}$ is flat over $\abs{\mathcal{A}}$} 
  \end{align*}
  \hspace*{\parindent} Finally, for $f: \mathcal{A} \to \mathcal{A}'$ a morphism of artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebras, 
  \[\brac*{\mathcal{A}' \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_\mathcal{A} \mathcal{K}} \vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \simeq \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \brac*{\abs{\mathcal{A}'} \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\abs{\mathcal{A}}} \abs{\mathcal{K}}}\] as complexes of $\abs{\mathcal{A}'} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}-$modules, giving compatibility with artinian base change.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
\leavevmode
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item A pro-MTS is a pro-object in $\mathsf{MTS}_\mathbb{C}$. 
    \item A pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra is a $\mathcal{T}(0)$-algebra object in the category of pro-MTS. Equivalently, it's an inverse system of $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS algebras.
    \item A pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra $\mathcal{A}$ is complete, noetherian, artinian, or local when $\abs{\mathcal{A}}$ is as a $\mathbb{C}$-algebra. 
    \item If $\bm{\Lambda}$ is a pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra, we define a pro-$\bm{\Lambda}$-MTS-module to be a pro-$\bm{\Lambda}$-module object in $\mathsf{MTS}_\mathbb{C}$. 
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
In this paper, every pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra $\bm{\Lambda}$ is complete noetherian with 
  \[\bm{\Lambda} = \varprojlim_n \bm{\Lambda}_n, \hspace*{\parindent} \bm{\Lambda}_n \coloneq \bm{\Lambda}/\mathfrak{m}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{n + 1}\]
  where each $\bm{\Lambda}_n$ is an artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra, with quotients $\bm{\Lambda}_{n + 1} \to \bm{\Lambda}_n$ being morphisms in $\mathsf{MTS}_\mathbb{C}$. In this situation, a pro-$\bm{\Lambda}$-MTS-module can be defined levelwise. We can also define a formal scheme $\operatorname{Spf} \abs{\bm{\Lambda}}$.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{def:harmonic-decomp1}
  Let $\mathcal{E}$ be a complex $\mathcal{C}^\infty$-vector bundle on a complex manifold $X$ equipped with a flat $C^\infty$-connection $D$. For $h$ a hermitian metric on $\mathcal{E}$, we have a decomposition
  \[D = D_h + \Psi\]
  where $D_h$ is unitary with respect to $h$, and $\Psi$ is self-adjoint for $h$. Take a further type decomposition into $(1, 0)$ and $(0, 1)$ components
  \[D_h = \partial_h + \bar{\partial}_h, \hspace*{\parindent} \Psi = \theta + \theta^*\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} We say that $h$ is pluriharmonic if $(\theta + \bar{\partial}_h)^2 = 0$. A harmonic bundle $(\mathcal{E}, D, h)$, equivalently $(\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h, \theta, h)$, is then a smooth complex vector bundle $\mathcal{E}$ with a flat smooth connection $D$ and a pluriharmonic metric $h$.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
A harmonic bundle $(\mathcal{E}, D, h)$ can be recovered from the data $(\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h, \theta, h)$ since $D_h$ is the unique Chern connection on $(E, \bar{\partial}_h)$ compatible with $h$, and $\theta^*$ is just the adjoint of $\theta$.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
\label{def:harmonic-decomp2}
  Given a harmonic bundle $(\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h, \theta, h)$ with the above decomposition, the associated holomorphic flat connection is 
  \[\brac*{E^{\mathrm{DR}}, \nabla} = \brac*{(\mathcal{E}, D^{0, 1} = \bar{\partial}_h + \theta^*), D^{1, 0} = \partial_h + \theta}\]
  i.e., the holomorphic structure of $E^{\mathrm{DR}}$ is given by $\bar{\partial}_h + \theta^*$, and the holomorphic connection is given by $\partial_h + \theta$. The associated Higgs bundle is 
  \[\brac*{E^{\mathrm{Dol}}, \theta} = \brac*{(\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h), \theta}\]
  i.e., the holomorphic structure on $E^{\mathrm{Dol}}$ is given by $\bar{\partial}_h$ and the Higgs field is $\theta$.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
Let $(\bar{X}, D)$ be a smooth projective complex variety $\bar{X}$ with a simple normal crossing divisor $D$. A harmonic bundle $(\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h, \theta, h)$ on $X = \bar{X} \setminus D$ is tame if $(E^{\mathrm{Dol}}, \theta)$ is the restriction of a logarithmic Higgs bundle on $(\bar{X}, D)$. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} A tame harmonic bundle $(\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h, \theta, h)$ is purely imaginary (resp. nilpotent) if the eigenvalues of the residues of $\theta$ in a logarithmic extension $\brac*{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{Dol}}, \theta}$ are purely imaginary (resp. zero). We say that $(\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h, \theta, h)$ has unipotent local monodromy if the local system corresponding to $\brac*{E^{\mathrm{DR}}, \nabla}$ has unipotent local monodromy.
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
These are independent of the choice of log smooth compactification $(\bar{X}, D)$ of $X$, and of the logarithmic extension $\brac*{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{Dol}}, \theta}$.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:mochizuki-correspondence}
  Let $\brac*{\bar{X}, D}$ be a log smooth curve. There is an equivalence of categories, via purely imaginary tame harmonic bundles with unipotent local monodromy, 
  \[\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}: \mathsf{Conn}^{\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X}, D) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathsf{Higgs}_{\mathrm{nilp}}^{0, \mathrm{poly}}(\bar{X}, D)\]
  between semisimple logarithmic flat vector bundle with nilpotent residues, and degree 0 polystable logarithmic Higgs bundles with nilpotent residues.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
The resulting $\brac*{\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{P}^1}, \mathcal{D}}$ is the variation of pure twistor structures ($\mathbb{C}-$VTS) of weight 0 associated to the tame harmonic bundle $\brac*{\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h, \theta, h}$. We get the weight $k$ $\mathbb{C}$-VTS associated to $\brac*{\mathcal{E}, \bar{\partial}_h, \theta, h}$ by instead taking $\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{P}^1, k} \coloneq \mathcal{E} \boxtimes \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(k)$.
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
Every semisimple complex local system on $X$ underlies a purely imaginary tame harmonic bundle, hence a variation of pure twistor structures.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
\label{def:C-VMTS-def}
  A graded-polarized variation of mixed twistor structures ($\mathbb{C}$-VMTS) on $X$ is a triple $\brac*{\mathscr{V}, W_\bullet, \mathcal{D}}$ such that 
  \begin{itemize}
    \item $\mathscr{V}$ is an $\mathscr{A}_X$-module on $X_{\mathbb{P}^1}$;
    \item $\mathcal{D}: \mathscr{V} \to \mathscr{V} \otimes \Omega^1_{X_{\mathbb{P}^1}/\mathbb{P}^1}(1)$ is a $x_0 \partial_{X_{\mathbb{P}^1}/\mathbb{P}^1} + x_\infty \bar{\partial}_{X_{\mathbb{P}^1}/\mathbb{P}^1}$-connection with $\mathcal{D}^2 = 0$;
    \item $W_\bullet \mathscr{V}$ is a $\mathcal{D}-$flat increasing weight filtration of $\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{P}^1}$;
    \item each $\brac*{\mathrm{gr}^W_k \mathscr{V}, \mathrm{gr}_k^W \mathcal{D}}$ has a pluriharmonic hermitian metric $h_k$ making it a $\mathbb{C}-$VTS of weight $k$ associated to a purely imaginary tame harmonic bundle. 
  \end{itemize}
  Morphisms are $\mathscr{A}_X$-linear morphisms which preserve $W_\bullet$ and commutes with $\mathcal{D}$.
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
The data of a $\mathbb{C}$-VMTS gives a family of holomorphic $\lambda$-connections, as follows. Look at the chart $\mathbb{P}^1\setminus \set{\infty}$, with $x_\infty$ being the trivializing section for $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(1)$. The restriction of $(\mathscr{V}, \mathcal{D})$ to a fiber $\lambda = x_0/x_\infty$ gives a $C^\infty$-connection 
  \[D_{\lambda} \coloneq x_\infty^{-1} \mathcal{D}\vert_{X \times \set{\lambda}}: \mathcal{V}_\lambda \to \mathcal{V}_\lambda \otimes \Omega_X^1\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Taking the type decomposition $D_\lambda = D_\lambda^{1, 0} + D_\lambda^{0, 1}$, since $D_\lambda$ is a $\lambda \partial + \bar{\partial}$-connection, 
  \[D_\lambda^{0, 1}(fs) = fD_\lambda^{0, 1}s + \bar{\partial}f \otimes s, \hspace*{\parindent} D_\lambda^{1, 0}(fs) = fD_\lambda^{1, 0}s + \lambda \partial f \otimes s\]
  and if $f$ is holomorphic, $D_\lambda^{1, 0}(fs) = fD_\lambda^{1, 0}s + \lambda \mathrm{d} f \otimes s$. Then $D^{0, 1}_\lambda$ gives holomorphic structure on $\mathcal{V}_\lambda$, and $D_\lambda^{1, 0} D_\lambda^{0, 1} + D_\lambda^{0, 1} D_\lambda^{1, 0} = (D_\lambda^2)^{1, 1} = 0$ implies that $D^{1, 0}_{\lambda}$ is a holomorphic $\lambda$-connection with respect to $(\mathcal{V}_\lambda, D_\lambda^{0, 1})$.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
An $\mathscr{R}_{Y}$-triple $\brac*{\mathscr{M}', \mathscr{M}'', C}$ consists of two coherent $\mathscr{R}_{Y}$-modules and a sesquilinear pairing along $\abs{\lambda} = 1$, see \cite[Section 2.1]{mochizuki2015mixed}. Mochizuki's category $\mathsf{MTM}(Y)$ of graded-polarized mixed twistor $\mathcal{D}$-modules is a full subcategory of the category of filtered $\mathscr{R}_{Y}-$triples, defined by imposing certain technical conditions on the nearby and vanishing cycles, see \cite[Section 7.1 and 7.2]{mochizuki2015mixed}.
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
A polarized $\mathbb{C}-$VTS (resp. graded-polarized $\mathbb{C}-$VMTS) on $Y$ gives a smooth polarized pure (resp. graded-polarized mixed) twistor $\mathcal{D}$-module on $Y$. For the pure case, see \cite[Theorem 1.2]{mochizuki2003asymptotic}, and for the mixed case, see \cite[Proposition 10.2.11 and 10.3.1]{mochizuki2015mixed} applied to empty boundary.
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
The category $\mathsf{MTM}$ is abelian \cite[Proposition 7.2.5]{mochizuki2015mixed} and enjoys nice functorialities. In particular, there are formalisms of derived pushforward, tensor products, and internal Homs \cite[Section 14.3]{mochizuki2015mixed}.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
Let $(\bar{X}, D)$ be a projective log smooth curve with $X = \bar{X} \setminus D$, an admissible variation of mixed twistor structure ($\mathbb{C}$-AVMTS) on $X$ is a mixed twistor $\mathcal{D}-$module on $\bar{X}$ whose restriction to $X$ underlies a tame purely imaginary graded-polarizable $\mathbb{C}-$VMTS.
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
A $\mathbb{C}-$VTS, associated to a tame purely imaginary harmonic bundle, has a canonical admissible extension \cite[Theorem 19.2]{mochizuki2003asymptotic}.
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- definition-14: body_md -->
For a $\mathbb{C}$-algebra $A$, an $A$-local system is a locally constant sheaf of finitely generated $A$-modules. We say that a $A$-local system is free if its stalks are finite free $A$-modules.
<!-- end definition-14 -->
<!-- definition-15: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{A}$ be an artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra. An $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS is an AVMTS $\mathscr{T}$ on $\bar{X}$ with a $\mathbb{C}$-VMTS $\mathscr{V} = j^*\mathscr{T}$ and an action morphism $\mathcal{A} \otimes_{\mathcal{T}(0)} \mathscr{V} \to \mathscr{V}$ which satisfies the unit and associativity diagrams. The underlying local system of $\mathscr{V}$ is a $\abs{\mathcal{A}}-$local system. It's free if the underlying $\abs{\mathcal{A}}-$local system is. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} If $\bm{\Lambda}$ is a pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra, then a pro-$\bm{\Lambda}-$AVMTS is of the form 
  \[\mathscr{V} = \varprojlim_n \mathscr{V}_n, \hspace*{\parindent} \mathscr{V}_n = \mathscr{V}/\mathfrak{m}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{n + 1}\mathscr{V}\]
  where $\mathscr{V}$ has an underlying $\abs{\bm{\Lambda}}-$local system, with each $\mathscr{V}_n$ a $\mathbb{C}$-AVMTS for which the quotient map $\mathscr{V}_{n + 1} \to \mathscr{V}_n$ is a morphism of $\mathbb{C}$-AVMTS, and for which $\bm{\Lambda}/\mathfrak{m}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{n + 1} \subset \mathrm{End}(\mathscr{V}_n)$ is a sub $\mathbb{C}-$MTS. We have a similar notion of freeness here.
<!-- end definition-15 -->
<!-- definition-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:artinian-coeff-base-change}
  It suffices to make sense of the tensor product $\mathcal{A}' \otimes_{\mathcal{A}}$ for $\mathcal{A} \xrightarrow{\phi} \mathcal{A}'$ a morphism in $\mathsf{MTS\text{-}Art}$. For an $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS $\mathscr{V}$, define 
  \[\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{A}'} = \mathcal{A}' \otimes_{\mathcal{A}} \mathscr{V} = \operatorname{coker}\brac*{\mathcal{A}' \otimes \mathcal{A} \otimes \mathscr{V} \xrightarrow{\alpha - \beta} \mathcal{A}' \otimes \mathscr{V}}\]
  where $\alpha: \mathcal{A}' \otimes \mathcal{A} \otimes \mathscr{V} \xrightarrow{1 \otimes \phi \otimes 1} \mathcal{A}' \otimes \mathcal{A}' \otimes \mathscr{V} \xrightarrow{\mu_{\mathcal{A}'} \otimes 1} \mathcal{A}' \otimes \mathscr{V}$, and $\beta: \mathcal{A}' \otimes (\mathcal{A} \otimes \mathscr{V}) \to \mathcal{A}' \otimes \mathscr{V}$. The resulting $\mathcal{A}' \otimes_{\mathcal{A}} \mathscr{V}$ is still an AVMTS since the category of $\mathbb{C}$-AVMTS is abelian. Fiberwise this is just the regular tensor product.
<!-- end definition-16 -->
<!-- definition-17: body_md -->
\label{def:AVMTS-extension}
  Suppose that $\mathscr{T}$ is a free $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS with unipotent local monodromy, then one has a notion of functorial Deligne's extension $(\mathscr{V}, W_\bullet \mathscr{V}, \bar{\mathscr{V}}, \bar{\mathscr{V}}', \mathcal{D})$ for $(\mathscr{V}, W_\bullet, \mathcal{D}) = j^*\mathscr{T}$ as well, see \cite[Lemma 8.7 and Corollary 8.8]{bakker2024linear}. We can view $(\mathscr{V}, \mathcal{D})$ as a family of $\lambda-$connections (see the remark following \cref{def:C-VMTS-def}), and $\bar{\mathscr{V}}$ and $\bar{\mathscr{V}}'$ are logarithmic extensions on the charts $\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda, \mathbb{A}^1_{\lambda^{-1}}$, respectively. The $\mathcal{A}$-action on $\mathscr{V}$ extends to $\abs{\mathcal{A}} \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}$ and $\abs{\mathcal{A}} \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_{\lambda^{-1}}}$-actions on $\bar{\mathscr{V}}$ and $\bar{\mathscr{V}}'$.
<!-- end definition-17 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
Over $\lambda \neq 0,  \infty$, these are just the normal Deligne's canonical extensions (we can scale each of these by $\lambda^{-1}$ to get a flat connection). Since $D \times \set{\infty}$ and $D \times \set{0}$ are codimension 2 in $\bar{X} \times \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$ and $\bar{X} \times \mathbb{A}^1_{\lambda^{-1}}$, these extensions $\bar{\mathscr{V}}, \bar{\mathscr{V}}'$ can be classified as the unique locally free logarithmic extensions which agree with the Deligne's canonical extensions over $\lambda \neq 0, \infty$.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:boundary-lattices}
  Let $\mathcal{A}$ be an artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra, and $\mathscr{V} = j^*\mathscr{T}$ a free $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS with unipotent local monodromy. Let $\mathscr{M}[*D]$ be the underlying $\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}}$-module of $j_*\mathscr{V}$. Then, for every $p \in D$ with local equation $(z = 0)$, then we have identifications
  \[V_{-1}\mathscr{M}[*D] = \bar{\mathscr{V}}, \hspace*{\parindent} V_0\mathscr{M}[*D] = z^{-1}\bar{\mathscr{V}}\]
  which are functorial in $\mathscr{V}$ and commute with artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra coefficient base change.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
For $\lambda_0 \in \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$, the superscript $^{(\lambda_0)}$ will denote taking a smaller family of $\lambda-$connections with $\lambda$ in a small neighborhood of $\lambda_0$. Let $\mathscr{M} = \mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}}(*D) \otimes_{\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}}} \mathscr{M}[*D]$ then \cite[Morphism (5.15)]{mochizuki2015mixed}, with $I = \set{p}, J = K = \emptyset$, simplifies to
  \[V_0\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}} \otimes_{V_0\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}}} \brac*{V_0\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}} \cdot Q_{b + 1}^{(\lambda_0)} \mathscr{M}} \to \mathscr{M}^{(\lambda_0)}[*D] \tag{2.3}\label{eq:map-def-V-filt}\]
  with the image defined to be $V_b^{(\lambda_0)} \mathscr{M}^{(\lambda_0)}[*D]$. Notice that $Q_{b}^{(\lambda_0)} \mathscr{M}$ is a logarithmic lattice \cite[Equation (5.2)]{mochizuki2015mixed}, and $V_0\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}}$ is generated by $z\lambda\partial_z$ which preserves logarithmic lattices. Furthermore, by \cite[Lemma 5.3.8]{mochizuki2015mixed} the morphism \eqref{eq:map-def-V-filt} is injective, and we get 
  \[V_{-1}^{(\lambda_0)} \mathscr{M}^{(\lambda_0)}[*D] = Q_0^{(\lambda_0)}\mathscr{M}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Finally, $Q_0^{(\lambda_0)}\mathscr{M}$ has nilpotent residues due to the fact that the relevant KMS datum is $(0, 0)$ and \cite[Section 5.1.2]{mochizuki2015mixed} , so it agrees with the Deligne extension for $\lambda \neq 0$. After gluing we get $V_{-1}\mathscr{M}[*D] = Q_0\mathscr{M}$ where $Q_0\mathscr{M}$ is a locally free extension of $\mathscr{V}$ which agrees with $\bar{\mathscr{V}}$ over $\lambda \neq 0$, i.e., they agree except for a codimension 2 locus. As a result they must actually agree, and we get the first equality; the second follows from \eqref{eq:V-filt-isoms}.\\
  \hspace*{\parindent} By functoriality of the V-filtration and the Deligne extension $\bar{\mathscr{V}}$, both are $A[\lambda]$-submodules of $\mathscr{M}[*D]$. So the first equality is that of $A[\lambda]-$submodules. Since this is an equality, to prove that the identification commutes with artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra base change, it suffices to show that is true for the Deligne extension $\bar{\mathscr{V}}$, i.e., for $\mathcal{A} \to \mathcal{A}'$ in $\mathsf{MTS\text{-}Art}$ with $A' = \abs*{\mathcal{A}'}$ we need $\bar{\mathscr{V}}_{\mathcal{A}'} = \bar{\mathscr{V}} \otimes_{A[\lambda]} A'[\lambda]$. Over $\lambda = 1$, the local system $\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{A}'}\vert_{\lambda = 1}$ is by construction (\cref{lem:artinian-coeff-base-change}) just $A' \otimes \mathscr{V}\vert_{\lambda = 1}$. Hence the new local monodromy operator around $p \in D$ is $T_{p, A'} = 1 \otimes T_{p, A}$. The same is true of $N_{p, A'} = \frac{-1}{2\pi i} \log T_{p, A'}$ since it's a finite polynomial of $T_{p, A'}$, and thus the exponential lattice also commutes with $A \to A'$. Over $\lambda \neq 0$, the Deligne extension is defined via scaling by $\lambda^{-1}$, so we get that $\bar{\mathscr{V}}_{\mathcal{A}'} = \bar{\mathscr{V}} \otimes_{A[\lambda]} A'[\lambda]$ over $\bar{X} \times \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda - D \times \set{0}$. Again, these are locally free and $D \times \set*{0}$ is codimension 2 in $\bar{X} \times \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$, so we get a global isomorphism. Functoriality of the Deligne extension over $\bar{X} \times \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$ gives agreement as $A'[\lambda]-$modules.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- definition-18: body_md -->
For a rank $r \geq 1$ and a base point $x \in X(\mathbb{C})$, we define the framed character variety to be the affine scheme
  \[R_B(X, x, r) \coloneq \operatorname{Hom}\brac*{\pi_1(X^{\mathrm{an}}, x), \mathrm{GL}_r}\]
<!-- end definition-18 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:Betti-MTS-algebra}
  Let $(V, \phi)$ be a framed rank $r$ local system underlying a $\mathbb{C}$-AVMTS $\mathscr{V}$, then the completed local ring $\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_B(X, x, r), (V, \phi)}$ has a pro$-\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra structure $\bm{\Lambda}_V$ with 
  \[\abs{\bm{\Lambda}_V} = \hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_B(X, x, r), (V, \phi)}\] 
  and the universal formal local system $\hat{V}$ underlies a free pro-$\bm{\Lambda}_V$-AVMTS $\hat{\mathscr{V}}$. The universal framing $\hat{\phi}: \hat{V}_x \to \hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_B(X, x, r), (V, \phi)} \otimes_\mathbb{C} \mathbb{C}^r$ is the fiber over 1 of a morphism of pro-$\bm{\Lambda}_V$-MTS-modules $\hat{\phi}: \hat{\mathscr{V}}_x \to \bm{\Lambda}_V \otimes_{\mathcal{T}(0)} \mathcal{T}(0)^{\oplus r}$.\\
  \hspace*{\parindent} These structures are uniquely determined by the following universal property: For any artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra $\mathcal{A}$ and any $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS $\mathscr{U}$ with a framing $\psi: \mathscr{U}_x \to \mathcal{A} \otimes_{\mathcal{A}} \mathcal{T}(0)^{\oplus r}$ which restricts to $(\mathscr{V}, \phi)$ mod $\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{A}}$, there is an unique morphism $\bm{\Lambda}_V \to \mathcal{A}$ of local pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebras such that 
  \[\brac*{\mathcal{A}, (\mathscr{U}, \psi)} \simeq \mathcal{A} \otimes_{\bm{\Lambda}_V} \brac*{\bm{\Lambda}_V, (\hat{\mathscr{V}}, \hat{\phi})}\]
  or, in other words, $\bm{\Lambda}_V$ represents the functor 
  \[\mathcal{A} \mapsto \{\text{framed free $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS deformations of $(\mathscr{V}, \phi)$}\}/\simeq\]
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- definition-19: body_md -->
The unipotent locus is defined to be the closed subscheme
  \[R_B^{\mathrm{unip}}(X, x, r) \coloneq V(\det(u - T_p^{\mathrm{univ}}) - (u - 1)^r = 0)\]
<!-- end definition-19 -->
<!-- defprop-1: body_md -->
\leavevmode
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item $\mathsf{R}_{\mathrm{Dol}}(r)$ is representable by a quasiprojective scheme $R_{\mathrm{Dol}}(\bar{X}, D, x, r)$ parametrizing degree 0 semistable logarithmic Higgs bundles with a framing. 
    \item $\mathsf{R}_{\mathrm{DR}}(r)$ is representable by a quasiprojective scheme $R_{DR}(\bar{X}, D, x, r)$ parametrizing degree 0 semistable logarithmic connections with a framing. 
    \item $\mathsf{R}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(r)$ is representable by a separated, locally of finite type over $\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$, algebraic space $R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X}, D, x, r) \xrightarrow{\lambda} \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$ parametrizing logarithmic $\lambda-$connections with a framing. 
  \end{enumerate}
  With no risk of confusion, we will simplify notation $R_{\Box}(r) \coloneq R_{\Box}(\bar{X}, D, x, r)$ for $\Box \in \set*{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathrm{Hod}, \mathrm{DR}}$.
<!-- end defprop-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
For (1), see \cite[Theorem 7.2]{bakker2024linear} and \cite[Theorem 4.10]{simpson1994moduli}. For (2), see \cite[Theorem 3.3]{nitsure1993moduli}. Finally, for (3), see \cite[Proposition 2.1]{simpson2022twistor}
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
The restriction to $\lambda = 1, 0$ of $R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X}, D, x, r)$ gives all logarithmic connections and all logarithmic Higgs bundles, respectively. These are bigger than $R_\mathrm{DR}(r)$ and $R_\mathrm{Dol}(r)$. However, we do have 
  \[\brac*{R_\mathrm{Hod}(r)\vert_{\lambda = 0}}^{\mathrm{semistable}, \deg = 0} = R_\mathrm{Dol}(r), \hspace*{\parindent} \brac*{R_\mathrm{Hod}(r)\vert_{\lambda = 1}}^{\mathrm{semistable}, \deg = 0} = R_\mathrm{DR}(r) \tag{3.2}\label{eq:restriction-of-Hod}\]
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
Notice that we have an algebraic $\mathrm{GL}_r$ action on all these moduli spaces. For a $T$-flat bundle $(E_T, \nabla_T, \phi)$, $g \in \mathrm{GL}_r(T)$ acts by $g \cdot (E_T, \nabla_T, \phi) \coloneq (E_T, \nabla_T, g \circ \phi)$. The action Dolbeault and Hodge spaces are similarly defined, and the action on the Betti space is via conjugation.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- definition-20: body_md -->
Similar to the Betti case, we can define the unipotent loci as closed sub-schemes/algebraic space
  \[R^{\mathrm{nilp}}_\Box(r) \subset R_{\Box}(\bar{X}, D, x, r)\]
  for $\Box \in \set*{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathrm{Hod}, \mathrm{DR}}$. Furthermore, we have restrictions
  \[R^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(r)\vert_{\lambda = 1} = R^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{\mathrm{DR}}(r), \hspace*{\parindent} \brac*{R^\mathrm{nilp}_\mathrm{Hod}(r)\vert_{\lambda = 0}}^{\text{semistable}, \deg = 0} = R^\mathrm{nilp}_\mathrm{Dol}(r)\]
<!-- end definition-20 -->
<!-- definition-21: body_md -->
If $\mathscr{V} = j^*\mathscr{T}$ is a $\mathbb{C}$-AVMTS, and $\mathscr{V}$ is $\mathbb{C}-$VTS on $X$, then the resulting map $\mathbb{P}^1 \to R^{\mathrm{nilp}, \mathrm{loc}}_\mathrm{DH}(\bar{X}, D, x, r)$ is called a preferred section.
<!-- end definition-21 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:Bett-DR-Dol-formal-isom}
  Recall the pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra $\bm{\Lambda}_V$ in \cref{thm:Betti-MTS-algebra}. 
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item \cite[Lemma 8.11]{bakker2024linear} We have a classifying map for the canonical logarithmic extension of the universal pro-$\bm{\Lambda}_V$-AVMTS $\hat{\mathscr{V}}$:
    \[\hat{s}_{(V, \phi)}: \mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{P}^1} \bm{\Lambda}_V \xrightarrow{\simeq} \widehat{R^{\mathrm{nilp}, \mathrm{loc}}_\mathrm{DH}(r)}_{s_{(V, \phi)}}\]
    which is an isomorphism onto the completion of $R^{\mathrm{nilp}, \mathrm{loc}}_\mathrm{DH}(r)$ along the preferred section. 
    \item Specializing at $\lambda = 1, 0$ we get 
    \[\mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{P}^1} \bm{\Lambda}_V \vert_{\lambda = 1} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \widehat{R_\mathrm{DR}(r)}_{s_1}, \hspace*{\parindent} \mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{P}^1} \bm{\Lambda}_V \vert_{\lambda = 0} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \widehat{R_\mathrm{Dol}(r)}_{s_0}\]
    where the targets are completions at $s_1$ and $s_0$ respectively. As a consequence of \cref{lem:affinesplitting}, we get a trivialization $\varphi_{\bm{\Lambda}_V}$ of $\bm{\Lambda}_V$, and  
    \[\tau_V \coloneq (\hat{s}\vert_{\lambda = 0})\circ \varphi_{\bm{\Lambda}_V, 0} \circ \varphi_{\bm{\Lambda}_V, 1}^{-1}\circ (\hat{s}\vert_{\lambda = 1})^{-1}: \widehat{R_\mathrm{DR}(r)}_{s_1} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \widehat{R_\mathrm{Dol}(r)}_{s_0} \tag{3.3}\label{eq:formal-DR-Dol-immersion}\]
    \item Let $H \subset \mathrm{GL}_r$ be an algebraic group fixing $[(V, \phi)] \in R_B(X, x, r)$. Then $H$ also fixes $[s_0] \in R_{\mathrm{Dol}}(r), [s_1] \in R_\mathrm{DR}(r)$, and induces $H^\mathrm{DR}$ and $H^\mathrm{Dol}$ actions on $\widehat{R_\mathrm{DR}(r)}_{s_1}$ and $\widehat{R_\mathrm{Dol}(r)}_{s_0}$ respectively. The isomorphism $\tau_V$ \eqref{eq:formal-DR-Dol-immersion}, as well as its inverse $\tau_V^{-1}$ are equivariant. 
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Recall that 
  \[\brac*{R_\mathrm{Hod}(r)\vert_{\lambda = 0}}^{\mathrm{semistable}, \deg = 0} = R_\mathrm{Dol}(r), \hspace*{\parindent} \brac*{R_\mathrm{Hod}(r)\vert_{\lambda = 1}}^{\mathrm{semistable}, \deg = 0} = R_\mathrm{DR}(r)\]
  and $s_0, s_1$ are polystable bundles of degree 0. Since semistability and degree 0 are open conditions, we get the following identifications of completions
  \[\widehat{R^{\mathrm{nilp}, \mathrm{loc}}_\mathrm{DH}(r)}_{s_{(V, \phi)}} \vert_{\lambda = 1} \simeq \widehat{R_\mathrm{DR}(r)}_{s_1}, \hspace*{\parindent} \widehat{R^{\mathrm{nilp}, \mathrm{loc}}_\mathrm{DH}(r)}_{s_{(V, \phi)}} \vert_{\lambda = 0} \simeq \widehat{R_\mathrm{Dol}(r)}_{s_0}\]
  giving (2). \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} For (3), let's first prove that $H$ fixes $[s_0]$ and $[s_1]$. Let $(E, \nabla, \phi)$ be the framed flat bundle on $X$ associated to $(V, \phi)$, $V$ is the sheaf of flat sections of $(E, \nabla)$ and we can canonically identify the fibers $V_x \simeq E_x$ thus justifying the same framing $\phi$. Furthermore if $(\bar{E}, \nabla)$ is the Deligne extension then the framing at $x \notin D$ is the same $\phi$ (so $s_1 = (\bar{E}, \nabla, \phi)$). Suppose that $g \in H$ fixes $[(V, \phi)]$, then $g_x \coloneq \phi^{-1} \circ g \circ \phi: V_x \to V_x$ commutes with all monodromy operators thus extends to an automorphism $a_g: V \xrightarrow{\simeq} V$ satisfying $\phi \circ a_g = g \circ \phi$. Now, $g_x$ commutes with local monodromy hence it preserves the Deligne's lattice, thus $a_g$ extends to an automorphism $\bar{a}_g: \bar{E} \xrightarrow{} \bar{E}$. And since the framing is the same, $\bar{a}_g: (\bar{E}, \nabla, \phi) \xrightarrow{\simeq} (\bar{E}, \nabla, g \circ \phi)$, i.e., $g$ fixes $[s_1]$. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Via \cref{thm:mochizuki-correspondence}, we get an automorphism $\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{a}_g)$ of $\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \nabla)$. The functor is defined via a tame nilpotent harmonic bundle $\mathcal{E}$ so we have $\bar{E}_x = \mathcal{E}_x = \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \nabla)_x$. It follows that $g$ fixes $[s_0] = [(\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \nabla), \phi)]$. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Since $(V, \phi)$ is semisimple, we get an automorphism $a_g$ of the weight 0 $\mathbb{C}$-VTS $\mathscr{V}$ as well, thus the change of framing $(\mathscr{U}, \psi) \mapsto (\mathscr{U}, g \circ \psi)$ gives an automorphism of the functor of framed free AVMTS deformations of $(\mathscr{V}, \phi)$. By \cref{thm:Betti-MTS-algebra}, we get an automorphism $\tilde{a}_g: \bm{\Lambda}_V \simeq \bm{\Lambda}_V$ of pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebras. We have a commutative diagram 
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{P}^1}\bm{\Lambda}_V} && {\mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{P}^1}\bm{\Lambda}_V} \\
	\\
	{R_\mathrm{DH}^{\mathrm{nilp}, \mathrm{loc}}(r)} && {R_\mathrm{DH}^{\mathrm{nilp}, \mathrm{loc}}(r)}
	\arrow["{c_g}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\hat{s}}"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["{\hat{s}}", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["g", from=3-1, to=3-3]
  \end{tikzcd}\]
  since change of framing commutes with logarithmic extension. By functoriality of \cref{lem:affinesplitting}, we have 
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{A}^1}\brac*{\bm{\Lambda}_V \vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}}} && {\mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{A}^1}\brac*{\bm{\Lambda}_V \vert_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}}} \\
	\\
	{\mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{A}^1} \brac*{\hat{O}_{R_B, (V, \phi)} \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}}} && {\mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{A}^1} \brac*{\hat{O}_{R_B, (V, \phi)} \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}}}
	\arrow["{c_g}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\varphi_{\bm{\Lambda}_V}}", from=3-1, to=1-1]
	\arrow["g", from=3-1, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\varphi_{\bm{\Lambda}_V}}"', from=3-3, to=1-3]
\end{tikzcd}\]
  and together they give the desired equivariance of $\tau_V$ and $\tau_V^{-1}$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- definition-22: body_md -->
A complex $K^\bullet \in D^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{O}_B)$ is perfect if, affine-locally on $U \subseteq B$, $K^\bullet$ is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex of finite rank locally free $\mathcal{O}_U$-modules. We denote $\mathcal{H}^0(K^\bullet) \in \mathrm{QCoh}(B)$ to be the zeroth cohomology object of $K^\bullet$.
<!-- end definition-22 -->
<!-- definition-23: body_md -->
For $K^\bullet \in D^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{O}_B)$ a perfect complex, denote 
  \[M^0(K^\bullet) \coloneq \operatorname{Spec}_B \mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_B}(\mathcal{H}^0((K^\bullet)^\vee))\] 
  where $(K^\bullet)^\vee \coloneq \mathrm{R}\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathcal{O}_B}(K^\bullet, \mathcal{O}_B)$.
<!-- end definition-23 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:relative-hom-scheme-lemma}
  Let $f: T \to B$ be a morphism of schemes and $K^\bullet \in D^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{O}_B)$ a perfect complex, then we have functorial bijections
  \[\operatorname{Hom}_B(T, M^0(K^\bullet)) \simeq \mathbb{H}^0(T, \mathrm{L}f^*K^\bullet)\]
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
By the proof of \cite[\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/08JX}{Tag 08JX}]{stacks-project}, $(T \xrightarrow{f} B) \mapsto \mathbb{H}^0(T, \mathrm{L}f^*K^\bullet)$ is a fppf sheaf. On the other hand, $(T \xrightarrow{f} B) \mapsto \operatorname{Hom}_B(T, M^0(K))$ is clearly a fpqc sheaf (representable by $M^0(K)$), so by \cite[\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/04U0}{Tag 04U0}]{stacks-project}, we can reduce to the case where $B = \operatorname{Spec} R$ and $K^\bullet$ is quasi-isomorphic to $(P^\bullet, \mathrm{d}^\bullet)$ a complex of projective $R-$modules with no negative term. Then 
  \[\mathcal{H}^0((K^\bullet)^\vee) = \operatorname{coker}\brac*{P^{1, \vee} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}^{0, \vee}} P^{0, \vee}}\]
  and 
  \begin{align*}
    \operatorname{Hom}_B(T, M^0(K^\bullet)) &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_B-\mathrm{alg}}\brac*{\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_B} \brac*{\operatorname{coker}\brac*{P^{1, \vee} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}^{0, \vee}} P^{0, \vee}}}, f_*\mathcal{O}_T}\\
    &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_T-\mathrm{alg}}\brac*{f^*\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_B} \brac*{\operatorname{coker}\brac*{P^{1, \vee} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}^{0, \vee}} P^{0, \vee}}}, \mathcal{O}_T}\\
    &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_T-\mathrm{alg}}\brac*{\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_B} \brac*{f^*\operatorname{coker}\brac*{P^{1, \vee} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}^{0, \vee}} P^{0, \vee}}}, \mathcal{O}_T}\\
    &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_T} \brac*{f^*\operatorname{coker}\brac*{P^{1, \vee} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}^{0, \vee}} P^{0, \vee}}, \mathcal{O}_T}\\
    &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_T} \brac*{\operatorname{coker}\brac*{f^*P^{1, \vee} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}^{0, \vee}} f^*P^{0, \vee}}, \mathcal{O}_T}\\
    &\simeq H^0\brac*{T, \ker\brac*{f^*P^0 \xrightarrow{f^*\mathrm{d}^0} f^*P^1}}\\
    &\simeq \ker\brac*{H^0(T, f^*P^0) \xrightarrow{H^0(f^*\mathrm{d}^0)} H^0(T, f^*P^1)} \simeq \mathbb{H}^0(T, \mathrm{L}f^* K^\bullet)
  \end{align*}
  where all these isomorphisms are functorial in $T \to B$.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- definition-24: body_md -->
For $\pi: M^0_B(H^\bullet) \to B$, the universal section is $u \in \mathbb{H}^0(M^0_B(K^\bullet), \mathrm{L}\pi^*K^\bullet)$ corresponding to the identity in $\operatorname{Hom}_B(M^0_B(K^\bullet), M^0_B(K^\bullet))$.
<!-- end definition-24 -->
<!-- coroll-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:relative-hom-and-restriction}
  For $\phi: B' \to B$, there is a canonical isomorphism 
  \[M^0_B(K^\bullet) \times_B B' \xrightarrow{\simeq} M^0_{B'}(\mathrm{L}\phi^* K^\bullet)\]
  which is compatible with universal sections, i.e., for $\pi': M^0_{B'}(\mathrm{L}\phi^* K^\bullet) \to B', \pi: M^0_B(K^\bullet) \to B$, and universal sections $u' \in \mathbb{H}^0\brac*{M^0_{B'}(\mathrm{L}\phi^* K^\bullet), \mathrm{L}(\phi \circ \pi)^* K^\bullet}, u \in \mathbb{H}^0\brac*{M^0_B(K^\bullet), \mathrm{L}\pi^*K^\bullet}$, we have $u' = \mathrm{L}\phi^*(u)$.
<!-- end coroll-1 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
For $T \xrightarrow{f'} B'$, denote $f: T \xrightarrow{f'} B' \xrightarrow{\phi} B$. We have 
  \[\operatorname{Hom}_{B'}(T, M^0_B(K^\bullet) \times_B B') \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_B(T, M_B^0(K^\bullet)) \simeq \mathbb{H}^0(T, \mathrm{L}f^*K^\bullet)\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} On the other hand, 
  \[\operatorname{Hom}_{B'}(T, M^0_{B'}(\mathrm{L}\phi^* K^\bullet)) \simeq \mathbb{H}^0\brac*{T, \mathrm{L}(f')^*(\mathrm{L}\phi^* K^\bullet)} = \mathbb{H}^0\brac*{T, \mathrm{L}f^*K^\bullet}\]
  so by Yoneda we get both statements.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- coroll-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:MTS-enhancement-relative-hom-representability}
  Let $\mathcal{A}$ be an artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra, and $\mathcal{K} \in D^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-module})$ is perfect. Write $\mathcal{K}^\vee \coloneq \mathrm{R}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{K}, \mathcal{A})$. Then for every morphism of artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebras $\mathcal{A} \to \mathcal{B}$ we have a bijection 
  \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-alg}}(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{A}} \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}^\vee), \mathcal{B}) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{B}\mathrm{-MTS-module})}(\mathcal{B}, \mathcal{B} \otimes_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{L}} \mathcal{K})\]
  which is functorial in $\mathcal{B}$.
<!-- end coroll-2 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
First,
  \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-alg}}(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{A}} \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}^\vee), \mathcal{B}) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-module}}(\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}^\vee), \mathcal{B})\]
  and since $\mathcal{K}^\vee \in D^{\leq 0}$, we have by degree reason
  \[\operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-module})}(\mathcal{K}^\vee, \mathcal{B}) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-module}}(\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}^\vee), \mathcal{B})\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Finally, by push-pull adjunction and duality we get 
  \begin{align*}
    \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-alg}}(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{A}} \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}^\vee), \mathcal{B}) &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-module})}(\mathcal{K}^\vee, \mathcal{B})\\
    &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{B}\mathrm{-MTS-module})}(\mathcal{B} \otimes_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{K}^\vee, \mathcal{B})\\
    &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{B}\mathrm{-MTS-module})}((\mathcal{B} \otimes_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{K})^\vee, \mathcal{B})\\
    &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{B}\mathrm{-MTS-module})}(\mathcal{B}, \mathcal{B} \otimes_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{L}} \mathcal{K})
  \end{align*}
  as desired.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- definition-25: body_md -->
Suppose that $\bm{\Lambda} = \varprojlim \bm{\Lambda}_n$ is a pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra, with $\Lambda = \abs*{\bm{\Lambda}}$. Let $B = \mathrm{Spf}\ \Lambda$, $\mathcal{K} = \set*{\mathcal{K}_n} \in D^{\geq 0}(\bm{\Lambda}_n\text{-MTS-module})$. Then, for $K \coloneq \abs*{\mathcal{K}}$, one can form a formal scheme $M^0_B(K) \to B$. By the previous discussion, a $\mathbb{C}$-point of $M^0_B(K)$ lying over $\operatorname{Spec} \mathbb{C} \xrightarrow{b} B$ is the same as a morphism $\mathbb{C} \to K \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\Lambda} \mathbb{C}$. We say that this is a Tate point if it underlies a morphism in $D(\mathbb{C}-\text{MTS})$
  \[\mathcal{T}(0) \to \mathcal{K} \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bm{\Lambda}} \mathcal{T}(0)\]
<!-- end definition-25 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:MTS-structure-relative-Hom-scheme}
  In the setting above, if $s$ is a Tate point of $M^0_B(K) \to B = \mathrm{Spf} \Lambda$, then the completed local ring $\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{M_B^0(K), s}$ underlies a pro-$\bm{\Lambda}$-MTS-algebra $\bm{\Gamma}$. This construction commutes with pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra coefficient base change, and fiber products over a pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra base.
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
We can work levelwise, and notice that the coordinate algebra of $M^0_{\operatorname{Spec} \Lambda_n}(K_n)$, which is $\mathrm{Sym}_{\Lambda_n} \mathcal{H}^0(K_n^\vee)$, underlies $\mathrm{Sym}_{\bm{\Lambda}_n} \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_n^\vee)$ which is a $\bm{\Lambda}_n$-MTS-algebra (the differentials of $\mathcal{K}^\vee$ are MTS-morphisms, and the category is abelian). A Tate point gives a morphism of $\bm{\Lambda}_n-$MTS-algebras $\mathrm{Sym}_{\bm{\Lambda}_n} \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_n^\vee) \to \mathcal{T}(0)$, hence is defined by a MTS-ideal (equivalently, the quotient still has a MTS structure). \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Coefficient base change follows from being bounded above by 0. Indeed, by the argument in \cref{lem:relative-hom-scheme-lemma}, we can pick a locally free quasi-isomorphic $P^\bullet$, where 
  \[\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_n^\vee) = \operatorname{coker}(P^{1, \vee} \to P^{0, \vee})\]
  hence given $\bm{\Lambda}_n \to \mathcal{A}$, using right exactness of tensor product we get
  \[\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_n^\vee) \otimes_{\bm{\Lambda}_n} \mathcal{A} \simeq \mathcal{H}^0((\mathcal{K}_n \otimes_{\bm{\Lambda}_n}^{\mathrm{L}} \mathcal{A}))\]
  
  Finally, notice that due to being bounded above by 0, $\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_n^\vee \oplus \mathcal{L}_n^\vee) \simeq \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_n^\vee) \oplus \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{L}_n^\vee)$ and we have
  \[M^0(K_n) \times_{\operatorname{Spec} \Lambda_n} M^0(L_n) \simeq M^0(K_n \oplus L_n)\]
  so compatibility with fiber products follows.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
In this setting where $\mathcal{K}_n \in D^{\geq 0}(\bm{\Lambda}_n-\text{MTS-module})$, restriction is exact, so 
  \[M_{\Lambda_n}^0(K_n) = M^0_{\bm{\Lambda}_n}(\mathcal{K}_n)\vert_{\lambda = 1}\]
  by \cref{lem:relative-hom-and-restriction}. The universal section on $M_{\Lambda_n}^0(K_n)$ is the restriction of the universal section on $M^0_{\bm{\Lambda}_n}(\mathcal{K}_n)$.
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:base-change-perfectness-Dol-DR-complexes}
  $K_\Box(E^\mathrm{univ}_\Box, F^\mathrm{univ}_\Box)$ is perfect in $D(\mathcal{O}_B)$ with no negative cohomology sheaf. For any $f: T \to B$, we have corresponding families $E_{\Box, T}, F_{\Box, T}$ on $\bar{X} \times T$ and we can form similar complexes. Then the formulation of $K_\Box$ commutes with base change, i.e., 
  \[K_{\Box, T}(E_{\Box, T}, F_{\Box, T}) = \mathrm{L}f^* K_\Box(E^\mathrm{univ}_\Box, F^\mathrm{univ}_\Box)\]
  As a consequence, $K_{\Box, T}(E_{\Box, T}, F_{\Box, T})$ is perfect in $D(\mathcal{O}_T)$ and base change works for $T' \to T$ over $B$ as well.
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
For the Dolbeault complex, this follows from \cite[\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0A1G}{Tag 0A1G}]{stacks-project} and that relative logarithmic Kahler differentials commutes with base change. For the De Rham complex, the same proof as in \cite[\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0FM0}{Tag 0FM0}]{stacks-project} works.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:moduli-of-triples-and-deformation}
  The schemes 
  \[R^\mathrm{trip}_\Box(r, s) \coloneq M^0(K_\Box^\mathrm{univ}) \to R_\Box^\mathrm{pair}(r, s)\]
  represents the functor of framed triples $(E, F, f)$ where $E, F$ are semistable degree 0 logarithmic flat connections (resp. logarithmic Higgs bundle), and $f$ is a flat/Higgs morphism. If $(E, F, f)$ is a closed point, and $A$ is artinian local, then we have the framed deformation functor
  \[\mathrm{Def}^\mathrm{trip}_{\Box, (E, F, f)} = \left\{
    \begin{array}{c|l}
      (E_A, F_A, f_A) & (E_A, F_A) \in \mathrm{Def}^\mathrm{pair}_{\Box, (E, F)}(A) \\
      & f_A \in \mathbb{H}^0(K_{\Box, A}(E_A, F_A)), \hspace*{\parindent} f_A \ \mathrm{mod}\ \mathfrak{m}_A = f
    \end{array}
  \right\}\Big/\simeq \]
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Let $B = R_\Box^\mathrm{pair}(r, s)$. By \cref{lem:relative-hom-scheme-lemma}, for $T \xrightarrow{f} B$,  
  \begin{align*}
    \operatorname{Hom}_B(T, R^\mathrm{trip}_\Box(r, s)) &\simeq \mathbb{H}^0(\mathrm{L}f^* K_\Box^\mathrm{univ})\\
    &\simeq \mathbb{H}^0\brac*{K_{\Box, T}(E_{\Box, T}, F_{\Box, T})}\\
    &\simeq \mathbb{H}^0\brac*{C_{\Box, T}(E_{\Box, T}, F_{\Box, T})}
  \end{align*}
  which gives the flat/Higgs morphisms between $E_{\Box, T}$ and $F_{\Box, T}$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:universal-MTS-framed-triple}
  Let $(E, \phi_E), (F, \phi_F)$ be framed semisimple complex local systems of rank $r, s$ on $X$, with unipotent local monodromy, and $f: E \to F$ a morphism of local systems. Fix the canonical weight 0 $\mathbb{C}$-VTS $\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}$; $f$ then extends to a morphism of $\mathbb{C}$-VTS. Denote 
  \[\Gamma_{E, F, f} \coloneq \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{B}}(r, s), (E, F, f)},\hspace*{\parindent} \Lambda_{E, F} \coloneq \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s), (E, F)} \simeq \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{\mathrm{B}}(r), E} \widehat{\otimes} \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{\mathrm{B}}(s), F}\]
  then we have a pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra $\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}$ on $\Lambda_{E, F}$ (\cref{thm:Betti-MTS-algebra}) and the following are true: 
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item There is a pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra $\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}$ with $\abs*{\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}} = \Gamma_{E, F, f}$, and $\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F} \to \bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}$ is a morphism of pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebras. 
    \item There is a universal triple $(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}, \widehat{f})$ where $\widehat{\mathscr{E}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}$ are free pro-$\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}$-AVMTS and $\widehat{f}$ a morphism of $\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}$-AVMTS, such that for every artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra $\mathcal{A}$, pullback gives a functorial bijection
    \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}(0)-\mathrm{alg}}(\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}, \mathcal{A}) \xrightarrow{\simeq} \left\{ 
      \begin{array}{c}
        \text{framed free }\mathcal{A}-\text{AVMTS triples }(\mathscr{E}_{\mathcal{A}}, \mathscr{F}_{\mathcal{A}}, f_{\mathcal{A}}) \\
        \text{with }f_{\mathcal{A}} \text{ a morphism of }\mathcal{A}-\text{AVMTS}\\
        (\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}, f) \simeq (\mathscr{E}_{\mathcal{A}}, \mathscr{F}_{\mathcal{A}}, f_{\mathcal{A}})\ \mathrm{mod}\ \mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{A}}
      \end{array}
    \right\}\Big/ \simeq\]
    \item Let $\bar{E}, \bar{F}$ be the Deligne extensions of $E, F$. Then we have identifications 
    \[\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}\vert_{\lambda = 1} \simeq \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}}, (\bar{E}, \bar{F}, f)}, \hspace*{\parindent} \bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}\vert_{\lambda = 0} \simeq \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \bar{F}, f)}\]
    and restrictions of $(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}, \widehat{f})$ give corresponding DR/Dol universal families. 
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Let $a_X: X \to \mathrm{pt}$ and $q: \bar{X} \times \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda \to \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$ be the projection. We will repeatedly make use of the following lemma 
  \begin{lemma}
    Let $\mathcal{A}$ be an artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra, and $\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}$ free $\mathcal{A}-$AVMTS. Then 
    \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-mod})}(\mathcal{A}, (a_X)_*\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})[-1]) \tag{3.5}\label{eq:adjunction-AVMTS-hom}\]
  \end{lemma}
  \begin{proof}
    Let $\mathscr{A} \coloneq (a_X)^*\mathcal{A}[1]$. Since $\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}$ are free we have $\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \simeq \mathscr{E}^\vee \otimes_{\mathcal{A}} \mathscr{F}$. By tensor-hom adjunction,
    \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{A}, \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))\]
    \hspace*{\parindent} We have a fully faithful embedding $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS $\hookrightarrow \mathcal{A}\text{-}\mathsf{MTM}$, and by push-pull adjunction in $\mathcal{A}\text{-}\mathsf{MTM}$ we get 
    \begin{align*}
      \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{A}, \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\text{-}\mathsf{MTM}}((a_X)^*\mathcal{A}[1], \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))\\
      &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-mod})}(\mathcal{A}, (a_X)_*\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})[-1])
    \end{align*}
    as desired. 
  \end{proof}
  Let $\bm{\Lambda}_{n} \coloneq \bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}/\mathfrak{m}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{n + 1}$, and $\Lambda_n \coloneq \abs*{\bm{\Lambda}_{n}}$. From \cref{thm:Betti-MTS-algebra}, we have universal pair $(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}})$ on $\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}$. Pulling back gives universal pair $(\mathscr{E}_n, \mathscr{F}_n)$ of $\bm{\Lambda}_n-$AVMTS. Let (see \cref{sec:The-comparison} for the relevant definitions)
  \[\mathcal{K}_n \coloneq (a_X)_* \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\bm{\Lambda}_n}(\mathscr{E}_n, \mathscr{F}_n)[-1], \hspace*{\parindent} K_n^{\mathrm{tw}} = \mathrm{R}q_* \mathrm{DR}_{\mathscr{R}}(\mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\bm{\Lambda}_n}(\mathscr{E}_n, \mathscr{F}_n)}[*D])\]
  then $\mathcal{K}_n \in D^{\geq 0}(\bm{\Lambda}_n-\text{MTS-module})$, with $K^{\mathrm{tw}}_n$ being the restriction of $\mathcal{K}_n$ to the chart $\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$. As a result, $\mathrm{L}\iota_c \mathcal{K}_n \simeq \mathrm{L}\iota_c K^{tw}_n$ for $c \in \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$. Let $\mathcal{K} = \set*{\mathcal{K}_n}$ then we can form 
  \[M^0_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}}(\mathcal{K})_{f} = \mathrm{Spf}_{\mathbb{P}^1}\ \bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}\]
  where $f$ is a morphism of $\mathbb{C}-$VTS hence a morphism of $\mathbb{C}$-AVMTS, and by \eqref{eq:adjunction-AVMTS-hom},
  \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathbb{C}\mathrm{-MTS})}(\mathcal{T}(0), (a_X)_*\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{T}(0)}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) \]
  $f$ is a Tate point. We then have that $\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}$ is a pro$-\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}$-MTS-algebra by \cref{lem:MTS-structure-relative-Hom-scheme}, giving (1). Next denote 
  \[(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_{\bm{\Gamma}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}_{\bm{\Gamma}}) \coloneq \bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f} \otimes_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}} (\widehat{\mathscr{E}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}})\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} By \cref{lem:MTS-enhancement-relative-hom-representability}, the universal section $\widehat{f} \in \operatorname{Hom}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}\mathrm{-alg}}(\mathrm{Sym}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}} \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}^\vee), \bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f})$, given by composing the universal section of $M^0_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}}(\mathcal{K})$ with the completion at $f$, corresponds to a morphism in $D(\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}\mathrm{-MTS-module})$
  \[\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f} \to \bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f} \otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}} \mathcal{K} \simeq (a_X)_* \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\Gamma_{E, F, f}}(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_{\bm{\Gamma}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}_{\bm{\Gamma}})[-1]\]
  where the last equality comes from $(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}})$ being free (hence $\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}$ commutes with tensor product). By \eqref{eq:adjunction-AVMTS-hom}, we get a corresponding morphism $\widehat{f}: \widehat{\mathscr{E}}_{\bm{\Gamma}} \to \widehat{\mathscr{F}}_{\bm{\Gamma}}$ of $\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}$-AVMTS. Now let $(\mathscr{E}_{\mathcal{A}}, \mathscr{F}_{\mathcal{A}}, f_{\mathcal{A}})$ be a framed free $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS deformation of $(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}, f)$. By \cref{thm:Betti-MTS-algebra}, there is a unique $\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F} \to \mathcal{A}$ such that 
  \[(\mathscr{E}_{\mathcal{A}}, \mathscr{F}_{\mathcal{A}}) \simeq \mathcal{A} \otimes_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}} (\widehat{\mathscr{E}}, \widehat{\mathscr{F}})\]
  and since $f_{\mathcal{A}}$ is a morphism of $\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}$, \eqref{eq:adjunction-AVMTS-hom} gives a corresponding 
  \[\mathcal{A} \to (a_X)_*\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}_{\mathcal{A}}, \mathscr{F}_{\mathcal{A}})[-1] \simeq \mathcal{A} \otimes_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}}^{\mathrm{L}} \mathcal{K}\]
  which, by \cref{lem:MTS-enhancement-relative-hom-representability}, is the same as an $\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}$-algebra morphism $\mathrm{Sym}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F}}\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}^\vee) \to \mathcal{A}$. Since $\mathcal{A}$ is artinian, hence complete, we get a factorization $\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f} \to \mathcal{A}$, finishing (2). \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Next, by \cref{thm:relative-DR-Dol-quasi-isom-with-coeff} and the De Rham comparison, we have 
  \[\mathrm{L}\iota_1 \mathcal{K}_n \simeq \mathrm{R}\Gamma(C_{\mathrm{DR}}(\mathscr{E}_{n, 1}, \mathscr{F}_{n, 1})) \simeq \mathrm{R}\Gamma(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\Lambda_n}(E_n, F_n))\]
  where $E_n, F_n$ are the universal local systems over $\Lambda_n$. Thus \cref{lem:relative-hom-and-restriction} gives that the fiber over 1 of $\bm{\Gamma}_{E, F, f}$ is isomorphic to $\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_B^{\mathrm{trip}}, (E, F, f)}$. The other two remaining identifications in (3) follow from a similar argument, where by \cref{thm:Betti-MTS-algebra},
  \[\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F} \vert_{\lambda = 1} \simeq \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{\mathrm{DR}}^{\mathrm{pair}}, (\bar{E}, \bar{F})}, \hspace*{\parindent} \bm{\Lambda}_{E, F} \vert_{\lambda = 0} \simeq \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \bar{F})}\]
  by \cref{thm:relative-DR-Dol-quasi-isom-with-coeff},
  \[\mathrm{L}\iota_1 \mathcal{K} \simeq \mathrm{R}\Gamma(C_{\mathrm{DR}}(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_1, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}_1)), \hspace*{\parindent} \mathrm{L}\iota_0 \mathcal{K} \simeq \mathrm{R}\Gamma(C_{\mathrm{Dol}}(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_0, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}_0))\]
  with $(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_1, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}_1))$ universal over $\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{\mathrm{DR}}^{\mathrm{pair}}, (\bar{E}, \bar{F})}$ and $(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_0, \widehat{\mathscr{F}}_0)$ universal over $ \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \bar{F})}$, and finally that the universal section commutes with restriction due to \cref{lem:relative-hom-and-restriction}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{A}$ be an artinian local $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra, and $\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}$ free $\mathcal{A}-$AVMTS. Then 
    \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-mod})}(\mathcal{A}, (a_X)_*\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})[-1]) \tag{3.5}\label{eq:adjunction-AVMTS-hom}\]
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Let $\mathscr{A} \coloneq (a_X)^*\mathcal{A}[1]$. Since $\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}$ are free we have $\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \simeq \mathscr{E}^\vee \otimes_{\mathcal{A}} \mathscr{F}$. By tensor-hom adjunction,
    \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{A}, \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))\]
    \hspace*{\parindent} We have a fully faithful embedding $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS $\hookrightarrow \mathcal{A}\text{-}\mathsf{MTM}$, and by push-pull adjunction in $\mathcal{A}\text{-}\mathsf{MTM}$ we get 
    \begin{align*}
      \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-AVMTS}}(\mathscr{A}, \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}\text{-}\mathsf{MTM}}((a_X)^*\mathcal{A}[1], \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))\\
      &\simeq \operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{A}\mathrm{-MTS-mod})}(\mathcal{A}, (a_X)_*\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})[-1])
    \end{align*}
    as desired.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- definition-26: body_md -->
Let $\mathsf{C}$ be either of the above categories. For $E \in \mathsf{C}$, denote $\mathrm{soc}(E)$ the sum of all simple subobjects of $E$, i.e., the unique maximal semisimple subobject. We have the socle filtration $S_\bullet E$
  \[S_0E = 0, \hspace*{\parindent} S_1E = \mathrm{soc}(E), \hspace*{\parindent} S_{i + 1}E/S_iE = \mathrm{soc}(E/S_iE)\]
  satisfying, for $f: E \to F$,
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item $f(S_iE) \subseteq S_iF$;
    \item $S_i(E \oplus F) = S_iE \oplus S_iF$;
    \item $\mathrm{gr}_S E$ is semistable;
    \item $(\mathrm{gr}_S g) \circ (\mathrm{gr}_S f) = \mathrm{gr}_S(g \circ f)$. 
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-26 -->
<!-- definition-27: body_md -->
Let $\pi: \bar{X} \times \mathbb{A}^1_\lambda \to \bar{X}$ be the projection. Define the Rees family associated to $S_\bullet E$ to be 
  \[\mathrm{Rees}_S(E) \coloneq \underset{i \in \mathbb{Z}}{\sum} t^i \pi^*(S_i E) \subset \pi^*E \otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}[t]} \mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}[t, t^{-1}]\]
  which is locally free on $\bar{X} \times \mathbb{A}^1_t$, and 
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item $\mathrm{Rees}_S(E)$ has a flat connection (resp. Higgs field) from restricting $\pi^*\nabla$ (resp. $\pi^*\theta$);
    \item $\mathrm{Rees}_S(E) \vert_{t = 1} \simeq E$ and $\mathrm{Rees}_S(E) \vert_{t = 0} \simeq \mathrm{gr}_S E$, equipped with either $\mathrm{gr}_S \nabla$ or $\mathrm{gr}_S \theta$;
    \item for $f: E \to F$ we have $\mathrm{Rees}_S(f): \mathrm{Rees}_S(E) \to \mathrm{Rees}_S(F)$ from restricting $\pi^*f$, and 
    \[\mathrm{Rees}_S(f) \vert_{t = 1} \simeq f, \hspace*{\parindent} \mathrm{Rees}_S(f) \vert_{t = 0} \simeq \mathrm{gr}_S f, \hspace*{\parindent} \mathrm{Rees}_S(g \circ f) = \mathrm{Rees}_S(g) \circ \mathrm{Rees}_S(f)\]
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-27 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
Define a morphism 
  \[\Delta_{\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}}(*D): \mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^\mathbb{C}(*D) \to \mathscr{M}_{\mathcal{A} \otimes \mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^\mathbb{C}(*D), \hspace*{\parindent} \phi \mapsto \brac*{a \otimes e \mapsto \phi(a \cdot e) - a\cdot \phi(e)}\]
  then $\Delta_{\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}}(*D)$ commutes with $A[\lambda]-$multiplication and $\lambda \partial_z$, hence a $\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}, A[\lambda]}(*D)$-linear. We have
  \[\mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}(*D) = \ker \Delta_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}(*D) = \mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^{A}(*D)\]
  and a corresponding $\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}, A[\lambda]}-$linear map $\Delta_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}[*D]$ for which 
  \[\mathscr{M}^A_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}[*D] \coloneq \mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}[*D] = \ker \brac*{\mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{T}(0)}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}[*D] \xrightarrow{\Delta_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}[*D]} \mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{T}(0)}(\mathcal{A} \otimes \mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}[*D]}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Furthermore, this is compatible with the V-filtration, 
  \[V_b\mathscr{M}^A_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}[*D] = \ker \brac*{V_b\mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{T}(0)}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}[*D] \xrightarrow{V_b\Delta_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}[*D]} V_b\mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{T}(0)}(\mathcal{A} \otimes \mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}[*D]}\]
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
That $\Delta_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}(*D)$ is $\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}, A[\lambda]}(*D)$-linear can be checked locally. We get the identification of $\mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}(*D)$ essentially by uniqueness of meromorphic extension. The existence of $\Delta_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}[*D]$ comes from \cite[Lemma 3.1.19]{mochizuki2015mixed} which also gives $A[\lambda]$-linearity. By \cite[Proposition 9.1.8]{mochizuki2015mixed}, $\Delta_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}[*D]$ is strict with respect to KMS-structure, hence the V-filtration. Finally, \cite[Lemma 3.1.20 and 3.1.21]{mochizuki2015mixed} gives the remaining identifications.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- coroll-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:boundary-Hom-lattices}
  We have functorial and artinian-base-change-compatible identifications 
  \begin{align*}
    V_{-1}\mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}[*D] &= \mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}_{A[\lambda] \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}_\lambda}}\brac*{\bar{\mathscr{E}}, \bar{\mathscr{F}}}\\
    V_0\mathscr{M}_{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}[*D] &= z^{-1}\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}_{A[\lambda] \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}_\lambda}}\brac*{\bar{\mathscr{E}}, \bar{\mathscr{F}}}
  \end{align*}
<!-- end coroll-3 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
By \cref{lem:boundary-lattices} and the previous proposition, it remains to show that Deligne's extension commutes with taking $\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}$, but this is straightforward from a local monodromy calculation.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:relative-DR-Dol-quasi-isom-with-coeff}
  There is a canonical quasi-isomorphism in $D^b(A[\lambda])$ 
  \[\Phi_{\mathscr{H}}^A: K^\mathrm{tw}_A(\mathscr{H}) \xrightarrow{\simeq} K^{\log}_A(\mathscr{H})\tag{4.1}\label{eq:twistor-log-quasi-isom}\]
  which commutes with artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra coefficient base change, and restricts to quasi-isomorphisms 
  \begin{align*}
    \mathrm{L}\iota_0^* K^\mathrm{tw}_A(\mathscr{H}) &\simeq R\Gamma\brac*{\bar{X}, \sqbrac*{\bar{\mathscr{H}}_{A, 0} \xrightarrow{\theta} \bar{\mathscr{H}}_{A, 0} \otimes \Omega^1_{\bar{X}}(\log D)}} \eqcolon K_{A, 0}^{\log}(\mathscr{H})\\ 
    \mathrm{L}\iota_1^* K^\mathrm{tw}_A(\mathscr{H}) &\simeq R\Gamma\brac*{\bar{X}, \sqbrac*{\bar{\mathscr{H}}_{A, 1} \xrightarrow{\nabla} \bar{\mathscr{H}}_{A, 1} \otimes \Omega^1_{\bar{X}}(\log D)}} \eqcolon K_{A, 1}^{\log}(\mathscr{H})\tag{4.2} \label{eq:log-Dol-DR-derived-fibers}
  \end{align*}
  in $D^b(A)$, over $\lambda = 0, 1$. For $\mathcal{A}$-AVMTS $\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}$, let 
  \begin{align*}
    \phi^0_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}: &\mathbb{H}^0\brac*{\bar{X}, \sqbrac*{\overline{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}_{A, 1} \xrightarrow{\nabla} \overline{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}_{A, 1} \otimes \Omega^1_{\bar{X}}(\log D)}}\\
    &\xrightarrow{\mathbb{H}^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_0^*\Phi)H^0(\varphi)\mathbb{H}^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_1^*\Phi)^{-1}}\mathbb{H}^0\brac*{\bar{X}, \sqbrac*{\overline{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}_{A, 0} \xrightarrow{\theta} \overline{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}_{A, 0} \otimes \Omega^1_{\bar{X}}(\log D)}}
  \end{align*}
  be the $A$-linear $\mathbb{H}^0$-isomorphism. Then it preserves identity and composition, i.e., for $\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G}$, 
  \[\phi^0_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{E}}(\mathrm{id}_{\mathscr{E}_1}), \hspace*{\parindent} \phi^0_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G}}(v \circ u) = \phi^0_{\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G}}(v) \circ \phi^0_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}(u)\]
  as well as zero, addition, and $A$-scalars. Equivalently, we have a commutative diagram of $A$-modules 
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{H^0\brac*{K^{\log}_{A, 1}(\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G})} \otimes_A H^0\brac*{K^{\log}_{A, 1}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}} && {H^0\brac*{K^{\log}_{A, 1}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G})}} \\
	\\
	{H^0\brac*{K^{\log}_{A, 0}(\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G})} \otimes_A H^0\brac*{K^{\log}_{A, 0}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})}} && {H^0\brac*{K^{\log}_{A, 0}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G})}}
	\arrow["\circ", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\phi^0_{\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G}} \otimes\phi^0_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}}"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["{\phi^0_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G}}}", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["\circ", from=3-1, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}\tag{4.3} \label{eq:H0-composition-compatibility}\]
  These $H^0$-compatibilities also commute with artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)-$MTS-algebra coefficient base change.
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Work locally near $p \in D$ with equation $(z = 0)$. First we check that the isomorphisms in {\eqref{eq:V-filt-isoms}}, 
  \[\lambda \partial_z: \mathrm{gr}^V_b \mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D] \xrightarrow{\simeq} \mathrm{gr}^V_{b + 1} \mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D] \hspace*{\parindent} b > -1, \hspace*{\parindent} z: V_0\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D] \xrightarrow{\simeq} V_{-1}\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D]\]
  are compatible with the $A[\lambda]-$action. Functoriality of V-filtration gives that each $V_b\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D]$ has a $A[\lambda]-$action. Functoriality of the prolongation gives that the $A[\lambda]-$action on $\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D]$ is $\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}}$-linear, hence must commute $\lambda \partial_z$ and multiplication by $\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}_\lambda}$ (in particular $z$).\\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Next, we have quasi-isomorphisms (by the previous isomorphisms involving V-filtration):
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{V_{-1}\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D]} && {V_{-1}\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D] \otimes \Omega^{1}_{\bar{X}_\lambda/\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}(\log D_\lambda)} \\
	\\
	{V_{-1}\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D]} && {V_{0}\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D] \otimes \Omega^{1}_{\bar{X}_\lambda/\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}} \\
	\\
	{\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D]} && {\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D] \otimes \Omega^{1}_{\bar{X}_\lambda/\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}}
	\arrow[from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{=}", from=3-1, to=1-1]
	\arrow[from=3-1, to=3-3]
	\arrow[hook, from=3-1, to=5-1]
	\arrow["{z \otimes z^{-1}}"', from=3-3, to=1-3]
	\arrow[hook, from=3-3, to=5-3]
	\arrow[from=5-1, to=5-3]
  \end{tikzcd} \tag{4.4}\label{eq:BBT-8.12-quasi-isoms}\]
  where the top square is $A[\lambda]$-linear since multiplying with $z$ is (and the $A[\lambda]$-action is on the left). The bottom square is $A[\lambda]-$linear because inclusions $V_b\mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D] \hookrightarrow \mathscr{M}^{A}_{\mathscr{H}}[*D]$ is. Now using \cref{lem:boundary-lattices}, we can identify the top row with $C^{\log}_A$, and apply $Rq_*$ to get $\Phi_A: K^\mathrm{tw}_A \xrightarrow{\simeq} K^{\log}_A$, which is $A[\lambda]-$linear, as desired. Now, $K^{\log}_A = \mathrm{R}q_* C^{\log}_A$ satisfies base change (same proof as in \cite[\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0FM0}{Tag 0FM0}]{stacks-project}) with restrictions $\iota_0, \iota_1$ thus giving \eqref{eq:log-Dol-DR-derived-fibers}. \\
  \\
  \textit{Compatibility with artinian $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebra coefficient base change:} 
  This follows from the identification of top row with $C^{\log}_A$, given by \cref{lem:boundary-lattices}, being compatible, and base changes 
  \[A'[\lambda] \otimes_{A[\lambda]}^{\mathrm{L}} K^{\log}_{A} \xrightarrow{\simeq} K^{\log}_{A'}, \hspace*{\parindent} A'[\lambda] \otimes_{A[\lambda]}^{\mathrm{L}} K^{\mathrm{tw}}_{A} \xrightarrow{\simeq} K^{\mathrm{tw}}_{A'}\]
  \textit{$H^0$-compatibility with composition:} For $\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}$, denote 
  \[C^V_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \coloneq \brac*{V_{-1}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^A[*D] \xrightarrow{\nabla_\lambda} V_{0}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^A[*D] \otimes \Omega^{1}_{\bar{X}_\lambda/\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda}}\]
  and $K^V_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \coloneq \mathrm{R}q_* C^{\log}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})$. We will use $\mathbb{H}^0$ interchangeably with $H^0\Gamma$, which is allowed due to the hypercohomology spectral sequence. In particular, for $c \in \set*{0, 1}$,
  \[\mathbb{H}^0\brac*{\bar{X}, \mathrm{L}\iota_c^*C^{\log/V}_A} = H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^{\log/V}_A), \hspace*{\parindent} \mathbb{H}^0\brac*{\bar{X}, \mathrm{L}\iota_c^*\mathrm{DR}_{\mathscr{R}}} = H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^* K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A)\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Let the quasi-isomorphism coming from \eqref{eq:BBT-8.12-quasi-isoms} and applying $\mathrm{R}q_*$ be named 
  \[K^{\log}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \xleftarrow[\simeq]{\nu_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}} K^V_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}) \xrightarrow[\simeq]{\tau_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}} K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})\]
  which induces $A$-isomorphisms 
  \[H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^{\log}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) \xleftarrow[\simeq]{\nu_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^c} H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^V_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) \xrightarrow[\simeq]{\tau_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^c} H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Restriction is exact for $C^{\log/V}_A$ and $K^{\log/V}_A$ since the terms are $\lambda$-flat, hence first two terms are just 
  \[H^0(K^{\log}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) = H^0\Gamma(\bar{X}, C^{\log}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})), \hspace*{\parindent} H^0(K^{V}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) = H^0\Gamma(\bar{X}, C^{V}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Notice then that $H^0(K^{\log}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))$ gives the space of horizontal log-flat sections at $c = 1$ and log Higgs sections at $c = 0$. We have compositions for these sections. Since $\nu^c_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}$ is identity on degree zero, and one can check that composition sends two flat $V_{-1}$-sections into a flat $V_{-1}$-section (with respect to the identification \cref{lem:boundary-Hom-lattices}), so composition makes sense for $H^0(K^{V}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))$ as well. 
  Define a \textit{composition law} on $H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(-, -))$ 
  \[\mu^c_{\mathrm{tw}}(v, u) \coloneq \tau^c_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G}}\brac*{(\tau_{\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G}}^{c})^{-1}v \circ \tau_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^{c}u}\]
  then we have a commutative diagram
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G})) \otimes H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))} && {H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G}))} \\
	\\
	{H^0(K^{\log}_{A, c}(\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G})) \otimes H^0(K^{\log}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))} && {H^0(K^{\log}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G}))}
	\arrow["{\mu_{\mathrm{tw}}^c}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\psi_{\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G}}^c \otimes \psi_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^c}"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["{\psi^c_{\mathscr{E},\mathscr{G}}}", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["\circ", from=3-1, to=3-3]\tag{4.5}\label{eq:c-tw-composition-diagram}
\end{tikzcd}\]
  by defining $\psi^c_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}} \coloneq (\tau^c_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}})^{-1} \circ \nu_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}^c: H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_c^*K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) \xrightarrow{\simeq} H^0(K^{\log}_{A, c}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}))$. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Next, let $a_X: X \to \mathrm{pt}$, and define $\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}} \coloneq (a_X)_*\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})[-1] \in D^b(\mathcal{A}-\text{MTS-module})$. Its $\mathscr{R}_{\bar{X}}-$module, on the $\mathbb{A}^1_\lambda$-chart, is $K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})$ (because non-proper pushforward is defined via proper pushforward on the compactification, see \cite[Section 14.3]{mochizuki2015mixed}). So each cohomology $\mathcal{H}^j(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}})$ is a twistor structure, hence locally free over $\mathbb{P}^1$, and for $c \in \set*{0, 1}$,
  \[\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}) \vert_{\lambda = c} \simeq H^0(\mathrm{L}\iota_a^*K^{\mathrm{tw}}_A(\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F})) \tag{4.6} \label{eq:H0-twistor-strict-restriction}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Now, on $\mathbb{A}_\lambda^1$-chart, we identified $\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}})$ with relative $\lambda-$flat $V_{-1}$-sections, and defined a composition law $\mu_{\mathrm{tw}}$. We also identified $V_{-1}$ piece with the logarithmic extension of the family of $\lambda$-connection coming from a AVMTS, so it must agree with composition on $X$. On the other hand, composition on $X$ is a morphism of $\mathcal{A}-$AVMTS (use \cref{def:C-VMTS-def} and the subsequent remark; composition is $D_\lambda$-flat and $A[\lambda]$-linear). So $\mu_{\mathrm{tw}}$ is the restriction of a morphism of $\mathcal{A}$-MTS-modules 
  \[\mu_{\mathrm{tw}}: \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G}}) \otimes \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}) \to \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G}})\]
