  with fiber over $\lambda = c$ as defined before. Functoriality of the trivialization in \cref{lem:affinesplitting} then gives 
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G}}) \vert_{\lambda = 1} \otimes_A \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}) \vert_{\lambda = 1}} && {\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G}}) \vert_{\lambda = 1}} \\
	\\
	{\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{F}, \mathscr{G}}) \vert_{\lambda = 0} \otimes_A \mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{F}}) \vert_{\lambda = 0}} && {\mathcal{H}^0(\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{E}, \mathscr{G}}) \vert_{\lambda = 0}}
	\arrow["{\mu^1_{\mathrm{tw}}}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["\varphi"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["\varphi", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\mu^0_{\mathrm{tw}}}", from=3-1, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}\]
  which, combining with \eqref{eq:c-tw-composition-diagram} and \eqref{eq:H0-twistor-strict-restriction}, gives \eqref{eq:H0-composition-compatibility}. Identity extends to identity on the logarithmic extension, so lies in $V_{-1}$, hence is fixed by the quasi-isomorphisms in \eqref{eq:BBT-8.12-quasi-isoms}. Thus identity is fixed throughout. Every diagram so far has been $A$-linear, hence we get all desired compatibilities.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
Compatibility of the quasi-isomorphism with arbitrary artinian base change, not just those coming from morphisms of $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebras, is due to base change preserving quasi-isomorphism. However, $H^0$-compatibility with composition does not commute with arbitrary base change, because $H^0$ does not commute with base change.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:formal-isom-triples-and-composition}
  For $(\bar{E}, \bar{F}, f)$ with $\bar{E}, \bar{F}$ polystable logarithmic flat connections and $f: \bar{E} \to \bar{F}$, we have an isomorphism 
  \[\Psi_{\bar E, \bar F, f}: \widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}, \bar{E}, \bar{F}, f} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \bar{F}, f)}\]
  Furthermore, if we let $\mu_{\mathrm{DR}}, \mu_{\mathrm{Dol}}$ be morphisms defined by composition on universal families, then there is a commutative diagram
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\widehat{Z}_{\mathrm{DR}, f, g}} && {\widehat{Z}_{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(f, g)}} \\
	\\
	{\widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}, \bar E, \bar G, g \circ f}} && {\widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar E, \bar G, g \circ f)}}
	\arrow["{\Psi_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}, f} \times \Psi_{\bar{F}, \bar{G}, g}}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["\mu_{\mathrm{DR}}"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["\mu_{\mathrm{Dol}}", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\Psi_{\bar{E}, \bar{G}, g \circ f}}", from=3-1, to=3-3]\tag{4.9} \label{eq:universal-composition-compatibility-diagram}
  \end{tikzcd}\]
  and analogous diagrams for direct sums, addition and scalar multiplication of universal maps.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
\textit{The isomorphism $\Psi$:} Let $A$ be artinian local. Denote the trivialization of \eqref{eq:pair-MTS-fiber-identification} 
  \[\tau_{E, F}: \widehat{R}^{\mathrm{pair}}_{\mathrm{DR}, \bar{E}, \bar{F}} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \widehat{R}^{\mathrm{pair}}_{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \bar{F})}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} By \cref{lem:moduli-of-triples-and-deformation}, an $A$ point of $\widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}, \bar E, \bar F, f}$ is 
  \[(\bar{E}_A, \bar{F}_A, f_A), \hspace*{\parindent} f_A \in \mathbb{H}^0(K_{\mathrm{DR}, A}(\bar{E}_A, \bar{F}_A)), \hspace*{\parindent} f_A \mod \mathfrak{m}_A = f\]
  since $(\bar{E}_A, \bar{F}_A)$ is a deformation of $(\bar{E}, \bar{F})$, we have a classifying morphism $\Lambda_{E, F} \to A$. This must factor through some quotient $\Lambda_n$, hence we can pullback \eqref{eq:nth-level-DR-Dol-cohomology-comparison} to get a quasi-isomorphism
  \[\Phi_A: K_{\mathrm{DR}, A}(\bar{E}_A, \bar{F}_A) \xrightarrow{\simeq} K_{\mathrm{Dol}, A}(\tau_{E, F}(\bar{E}_A, \bar{F}_A))\]
  which is independent of $n$ by base change compatibility. Then 
  \[((\bar{E}_A, \bar{F}_A), f_A) \mapsto \brac*{\tau_{E, F}(\bar{E}_A, \bar{F}_A), H^0(\Phi_A)(f_A)}\]
  with inverse constructed using $\tau^{-1}_{E, F}$ and $\Phi_A^{-1}$, gives a functorial bijection on Artinian points. By formal Yoneda \cite{schlessinger1968functors}, we get the isomorphism $\Psi_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}, f}$. Compatibility with universal families follows from Yoneda. \\
  \\
  \textit{Compatibility with composition, direct sum, addition, scalar multiplication:} Now let $E, F, G$ be the underlying local system for logarithmic flat connections $\bar{E}, \bar{F}, \bar{G}$, equipped with the canonical weight 0 $\mathbb{C}$-VTS. We then have a pro-$\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS deformation algebra $\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F, G}$ whose fibers over $\lambda = 1, 0$ give $\hat{B}_{\mathrm{DR}, \bar{E}, \bar{F}, \bar{G}}$ and $\hat{B}_{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}, \bar{F}, \bar{G})}$. Similar statement for universal families $\brac*{\hat{\mathscr{E}}, \hat{\mathscr{F}}, \hat{\mathscr{G}}}$. Let 
  \[\hat{Z}_{\mathrm{DR}} \eqcolon \mathrm{Spf}\ \Gamma\]
  then by \cref{thm:universal-MTS-framed-triple}, we have a pro-$\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F, G}$-MTS-algebra $\bm{\Gamma}$ with $\abs*{\bm{\Gamma}} = \Gamma$. Denote 
  \[\bm{\Gamma}_n \coloneq \bm{\Gamma}/\mathfrak{m}_{\bm{\Gamma}}^{n + 1}, \hspace*{\parindent} \Gamma_n = \abs*{\bm{\Gamma}_n}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} The morphism $\bm{\Lambda}_{E, F, G} \to \bm{\Gamma} \to \bm{\Gamma}_n$ is a morphism of $\mathcal{T}(0)$-MTS-algebras, hence pulling back universal families give $\hat{\mathscr{E}}_n, \hat{\mathscr{F}}_n, \hat{\mathscr{G}}_n$ all $\bm{\Gamma}_n$-AVMTS. Again let their $\lambda-$fiber be $\bar{E}_{n, \lambda}, \bar{F}_{n, \lambda}, \bar{G}_{n, \lambda}$. Since we are over $\bm{\Gamma}$ which also parametrizes deformations of $f, g$, there are universal morphisms 
  \[f_n \in H^0\brac*{K_{\mathrm{DR}, \Gamma_n}(\bar{E}_{n, 1}, \bar{F}_{n, 1})}, \hspace*{\parindent} g_n \in H^0\brac*{K_{\mathrm{DR}, \Gamma_n}(\bar{F}_{n, 1}, \bar{G}_{n, 1})}\]
  which are just the restriction of the universal morphisms $\hat{f}, \hat{g}$ along $\Gamma \to \Gamma_n$. Now by \cref{thm:relative-DR-Dol-quasi-isom-with-coeff} applied to $\bm{\Gamma}_n$, $H^0(\Phi_n)$ commutes with composition. Thus the restriction of the diagram \eqref{eq:universal-composition-compatibility-diagram} to each $\Gamma_n$ commutes. Since $\Gamma = \varprojlim \Gamma_n$, we get the desired diagram. Compatibility with addition and scalar multiplication follows from the same argument, along with the fact that $H^0(\Phi_n)$ is $\Gamma_n$-linear. Direct sum is analogous.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- prop-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:Rees-equivariance-formal-triples}
  The formal isomorphism $\Psi_{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}, f^{\mathrm{ss}}}$ in \cref{thm:formal-isom-triples-and-composition} is equivariant.
<!-- end prop-8 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
For artinian local ring $A$, we have by definition
  \[\Psi_{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}, f^{\mathrm{ss}}}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A, f_A) = (\tau_{E^{\mathrm{ss}}, F^{\mathrm{ss}}}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A), H^0(\Phi_A)(f_A))\]
  and $\tau_{E^{\mathrm{ss}}, F^{\mathrm{ss}}}$ is equivariant by \cref{thm:Bett-DR-Dol-formal-isom}. Notice that the $\gamma^{\mathrm{DR}}$ acts by conjugation on $\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_A(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A)$, hence by conjugation on the logarithmic De Rham complex. Equivariance of $\tau_{E^{\mathrm{ss}}, F^{\mathrm{ss}}}$ and functoriality of $\Phi_A$ then gives a commutative diagram 
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{K_{\mathrm{DR}, A}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A)} && {K_{\mathrm{Dol}, A}(\tau_{E^{\mathrm{ss}}, F^{\mathrm{ss}}}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A))} \\
	\\
	{\gamma^{\mathrm{DR}}(t) \cdot K_{\mathrm{DR}, A}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A)} && {\gamma^{\mathrm{Dol}}(t) \cdot K_{\mathrm{Dol}, A}(\tau_{E^{\mathrm{ss}}, F^{\mathrm{ss}}}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}_A))}
	\arrow["{\Phi_A}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\gamma_{\bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}}^{\mathrm{DR}}(t) \circ (-) \circ \gamma_{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}}^{\mathrm{DR}}(t)^{-1} }"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["{\gamma_{\bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}}^{\mathrm{Dol}}(t) \circ (-) \circ \gamma_{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}}^{\mathrm{Dol}}(t)^{-1}}", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\Phi_A}", from=3-1, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}\]
  hence $H^0(\Phi_A)$ is equivariant.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- definition-28: body_md -->
A $G$-equivariant quasicoherent $\mathcal{O}_B$-module on $B$ is a pair $(\mathcal{F}, \alpha)$ where $\mathcal{F} \in \mathrm{QCoh}(\mathcal{O}_B)$ and $\alpha$ is a $\mathcal{O}_{G \times B}$-linear isomorphism $\alpha: a^*\mathcal{F} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \pi^*\mathcal{F}$ such that the diagram
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{(\mathrm{id}_G \times a)^*\pi^*\mathcal{F}} && {\pi_2^*\mathcal{F}} \\
	\\
	{(\mathrm{id}_G \times a)^* a^*\mathcal{F}} && {(m \times \mathrm{id}_B)^*a^*\mathcal{F}}
	\arrow["{\pi_{12}^*\alpha}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{(\mathrm{id}_G \times a)^* \alpha}", from=3-1, to=1-1]
	\arrow[no head, from=3-1, to=3-3]
	\arrow[shift left, no head, from=3-1, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{(m \times \mathrm{id}_B)^* \alpha}"', from=3-3, to=1-3]
\end{tikzcd}\]
commutes in the category of $\mathcal{O}_{G \times G \times B}$-modules, where $\pi_2: G \times G \times B \to B, \pi_{12}: G \times G \times B \to G \times B$ are projections.
<!-- end definition-28 -->
<!-- definition-29: body_md -->
\leavevmode
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item A $G$-equivariant complex of $\mathcal{O}_B$-modules is a cochain complex $(K^\bullet, \mathrm{d}^\bullet)$ where each $K^i$ is a $G$-equivariant quasicoherent $\mathcal{O}_B$-module with $\alpha_i: a^* K^i \xrightarrow{\simeq} \pi^* K^i$, and each $\mathrm{d}^i$ is $G$-equivariant
    \[\alpha_{i + 1} \circ (a^* \mathrm{d}^i) = (\pi^* \mathrm{d}^i) \circ \alpha_i\]
    \item Let $Z$ be a $\mathbb{C}$-scheme with a trivial $G$-action, and $\mathrm{pr}_1: Z \times B \to B$ be the projection. Denote
    \begin{align*}
      \tilde{a} &\coloneq \mathrm{id}_Z \times a: G \times Z \times B \to Z \times B\\
      \tilde{\pi} &\coloneq \mathrm{id}_Z \times \pi: G \times Z \times B \to Z \times B
    \end{align*}
    the action and projection on $Z \times B$. A $G$-equivariant complex of $\mathrm{pr}^{-1}_1\mathcal{O}_B$-modules is a cochain complex $(K^\bullet, \mathrm{d}^\bullet)$ where each $K^i$ is a $G$-equivariant $\mathcal{O}_{Z \times B}$-module, and 
    \[\alpha_{i + 1} \circ \tilde{a}_{B}^* \mathrm{d}^i = (\tilde{\pi}_B^*\mathrm{d}^i) \circ \alpha_i\]
    where $\tilde{a}_B^*\mathcal{F} \coloneq \mathrm{pr}_{02}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{G \times B} \otimes_{\tilde{a}^{-1} \mathrm{pr}_1^{-1}\mathcal{O}_B}\tilde{a}^{-1} \mathcal{F}$, with $\tilde{\pi}_B^*$ similarly defined. 
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-29 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:equivariant-pullback}
  \leavevmode
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item Let $Y$ be a $\mathbb{C}$-scheme with a $G$-action. Let $f: Y \to B$ be a $G$-equivariant morphism, and $(K^\bullet, \mathrm{d}^i)$ a $G$-equivariant complex of $\mathcal{O}_Y$-modules (meaning each $\mathrm{d}^i$ is $\mathcal{O}_B$-linear). Then $f^* K^\bullet$ is a $G$-equivariant complex of $\mathcal{O}_Y$-modules. 
    \item Let $T$ be another $\mathbb{C}$-scheme with a $G$-action, and $g: T \to B$ a $G$-equivariant morphism. Form the fibred diagram 
    \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{Z \times T} && Z \times B \\
	\\
	T && B
	\arrow["{\mathrm{id}_Z \times g}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\mathrm{pr}_1}"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["{\mathrm{pr}_1}", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["g", from=3-1, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}\]
  Suppose that $(K^\bullet, \mathrm{d}^i)$ is a $G$-equivariant complex of $\mathrm{pr}_1^{-1}\mathcal{O}_B$-modules on $Z \times B$. Then 
  \[g_B^* K^\bullet \coloneq \mathrm{pr}_1^{-1}\mathcal{O}_T \otimes_{(\mathrm{id}_Z \times g)^{-1}\mathrm{pr}_1^{-1}\mathcal{O}_B} (\mathrm{id}_Z \times g)^{-1} K^\bullet\]
  is a $G$-equivariant complex of $\mathrm{pr}_1^{-1}\mathcal{O}_T$-modules. 
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
See \cite[\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/03LG}{Tag 03LG}]{stacks-project}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- prop-9: body_md -->
$C_{\mathrm{Dol}}(\bar{E}_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{univ}}, \bar{F}_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{univ}})$ is a $G$-equivariant complex of $\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X} \times R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)}-$modules. $C_{\mathrm{DR}}(\bar{E}_{\mathrm{DR}}^{\mathrm{univ}}, \bar{F}_{\mathrm{DR}}^{\mathrm{univ}})$ is a $G-$equivariant complex of $\mathrm{pr}_1^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{R_{\mathrm{DR}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)}$-modules. $K_{\Box}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\Box}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\Box}) = \mathrm{R}(\mathrm{pr}_1)_* C_{\Box}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\Box}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\Box})$ is a $G$-equivariant complex of $\mathcal{O}_{R_{\Box}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)}$-modules.
<!-- end prop-9 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Let's prove the first statement; the second one uses the same argument just with different linear structures. Let $a: G \times R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s) \to R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s), \pi: G \times R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s) \to R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)$ be the action and projection on $R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)$. Denote $\tilde{a} = \mathrm{id}_{\bar{X}} \times a, \tilde{\pi} = \mathrm{id}_{\bar{X}} \times \pi$ the action (trivial on $\bar{X}$) and projection on $\bar{X} \times R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)$. Notice that the $G$-action only changes the framings, not the underlying bundles, so if we forget about framing (which is fine since the complexes don't care about framing), uniqueness of universal bundle gives canonical isomorphisms of logarithmic Higgs bundles 
  \[\alpha_{\bar{E}}: \tilde{a}^*\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \tilde{\pi}^*\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}, \hspace*{\parindent} \alpha_{\bar{F}}: \tilde{a}^*\bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \tilde{\pi}^*\bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}\]
  which satisfies the cocycle condition because change-of-framing on $R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)$ just induces a change-of-framing on the universal bundles. This induces, by functoriality, an isomorphism
  \[\alpha_{\operatorname{Hom}}: \tilde{a}^* \mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}) \xrightarrow{\simeq} \tilde{\pi}^*\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}), \hspace*{\parindent} \phi \mapsto \alpha_{\bar{F}} \circ \phi \circ \alpha_{\bar E}^{-1}\]
  which satisfies the cocycle condition because $\alpha_{\bar{E}}, \alpha_{\bar{F}}$ do. Thus $\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}})$ is $G$-equivariant via $\alpha_{\operatorname{Hom}}$, and the remaining term of the complex
  \[\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}) \otimes \Omega^1_{\bar{X} \times R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)/R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s)}(\log D \times R_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}(r, s))\] 
  is equivariant via $\alpha_{\operatorname{Hom}} \otimes 1$. Since $\alpha_{\bar{E}}$ and $\alpha_{\bar{F}}$ are isomorphisms of Higgs bundles, they commute with the Higgs fields:
  \[(\alpha_{\bar E} \otimes 1) \circ (\tilde{a}^*\theta_{\bar{E}}) = (\tilde{\pi}^*\theta_{\bar{E}}) \circ \alpha_{\bar E}, \hspace*{\parindent} (\alpha_{\bar F} \otimes 1) \circ (\tilde{a}^*\theta_{\bar{F}}) = (\tilde{\pi}^*\theta_{\bar{F}}) \circ \alpha_{\bar F}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} We also have that the induced Higgs field on $\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\mathrm{Dol}})$ is 
  \[\theta_{\operatorname{Hom}}(\phi) = \theta_{\bar{F}} \circ \phi - (\phi \otimes 1) \circ \theta_{\bar{E}}\]
  and, by plugging in, we have that $(\alpha_{\operatorname{Hom}} \otimes 1) \circ \tilde{a}^*\theta_{\operatorname{Hom}} = \tilde{\pi}^*\theta_{\operatorname{Hom}} \circ \alpha_{\operatorname{Hom}}$. Thus $\theta_{\operatorname{Hom}}$ is $G$-equivariant. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} For the last statement, we can use base change 
  \[a^*K_{\Box} = a^* \mathrm{R}(\mathrm{pr}_1)_*C_{\Box} \simeq \mathrm{R}(\mathrm{pr}_{12})_* \tilde{a}^* C_{\Box} \simeq \mathrm{R}(\mathrm{pr}_{12})_*\tilde{\pi}^*C_{\Box} \simeq \pi^* \mathrm{R}(\mathrm{pr}_1)_*C_{\Box} \simeq \pi^* K_{\Box}\]
  and functoriality preserves the cocycle diagram.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:Gm-equiv-complex-A1-graded-free}
  Let $K^\bullet$ be a $\mathbb{G}_m$-equivariant complex of $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_t}$-modules. Suppose that $K^\bullet$ is perfect. Then $K^\bullet$ is equivariantly quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex of finite rank graded free $\mathbb{C}[t]$-modules 
  \[K^i \simeq \bigoplus_j \mathbb{C}[[t]](w_{ij})\]
  with degree 0 differentials $\mathrm{d}^i$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
$\mathbb{G}_m$-equivariant quasicoherent $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^1_t}$-modules are just graded $\mathbb{C}[t]$-modules. The category of graded $\mathbb{C}[t]-$modules have enough projectives, and perfectness gives bounded graded projective resolution. Finally, $\mathbb{C}[t]$ is graded-local, so by \cite[Proposition 1.5.15(d)]{bruns1998cohen}, graded projective $\mathbb{C}[t]$-modules are free.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:Gm-equiv-formal-arc-algebraization}
  Let $Y$ be a finite type $\mathbb{C}$-scheme with a $\mathbb{G}_m$-action. Let $y_0 \in Y$ be a fixed point which has an invariant affine neighborhood. Then we have a bijection
  \[\set*{\mathbb{G}_m-\text{equivariant }(\mathbb{A}^1_t, 0) \to (Y, y_0)} \xrightarrow{\simeq} \set*{\mathbb{G}_m-\text{equivariant }(\mathrm{Spf}\ \mathbb{C}[[t]], 0) \to (\widehat{Y}_{y_0}, y_0)}\]
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
We will apply this lemma only to $Y = R_{\mathrm{DR}/\mathrm{Dol}}, R_{\mathrm{DR}/\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{trip}}$ with $y_0$ either a polystable bundle or a triple $(\bar{E}, \bar{F}, f)$ with $\bar{E}, \bar{F}$ polystable. In the first case, by GIT theory we know that the existence of a $\mathrm{GL}_r$-invariant affine neighborhood is guaranteed. For the second case, notice that $R_{\mathrm{DR}/\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{trip}} \to R_{\mathrm{DR}/\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}$ is affine, so we can pull back the $\mathrm{GL}_r \times \mathrm{GL}_s$-invariant affine neighborhood around $(\bar{E}, \bar{F}) \in R_{\mathrm{DR}/\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}$.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Let $U = \operatorname{Spec} B$ be a $\mathbb{G}_m$-invariant affine neighborhood containing $y_0$, and $\mathfrak{m}_0 \subset B$ the maximal ideal corresponding to $y_0$. Let the $\mathbb{G}_m$-action on $U$ be denoted by $\gamma: B \to B \otimes_{\mathbb{C}} \mathbb{C}[u, u^{-1}]$, which is equivalent to a grading $B = \oplus_{m \in \mathbb{Z}} B_m$ where if $\gamma(b) = \sum b_n \otimes u^n$ then $b_n \in B_n$. This actions completes to $\hat{\gamma}: \widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{m}_0} \to \widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{m}_0} \widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}} \mathbb{C}[u, u^{-1}]$. Let $\widehat{\alpha}: \widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{m}_0} \to \mathbb{C}[[t]]$ be a $\hat{\gamma}$-equivariant arc, i.e.,
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{m}_0}} && {\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{m}_0} \widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}} \mathbb{C}[u, u^{-1}]} \\
	\\
	{\mathbb{C}[[t]]} && {\mathbb{C}[[t]] \widehat{\otimes} \mathbb{C}[u, u^{-1}]}
	\arrow["{\hat{\gamma}}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\widehat{\alpha}}"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["{\widehat{\alpha} \otimes \mathrm{id}}", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{t \mapsto t\otimes u}", from=3-1, to=3-3]
  \end{tikzcd}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Denote $\iota: B \to \widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{m}_0}$. For $b \in B_m$, $\hat{\gamma}(\iota(b)) = \iota(b) \otimes u^m$, so $\widehat{\alpha}(\iota(b)) \in \mathbb{C}[[t]]_m$. It follows that $\alpha \coloneq \widehat{\alpha} \circ \iota: B \to \mathbb{C}[[t]]$ has image contained in $\mathbb{C}[t]$, hence giving an algebraic arc. Uniqueness of $\alpha$ trivial.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:Gm-equiv-formal-complex-algebraization}
  Let $K^\bullet, L^\bullet$ be perfect $\mathbb{G}_m$-equivariant complexes on $\mathbb{A}^1_t$. We can assume that $K^\bullet, L^\bullet$ are both graded free by \cref{lem:Gm-equiv-complex-A1-graded-free}, and denote 
  \[\hat{K}^\bullet \coloneq K^\bullet \otimes_{\mathbb{C}[t]} \mathbb{C}[[t]], \hspace*{\parindent} \hat{L}^\bullet \coloneq L^\bullet \otimes_{\mathbb{C}[t]} \mathbb{C}[[t]]\]
  be the restriction to the formal neighborhood of $\mathbb{A}^1_t$ at 0. Then every continuous $\mathbb{G}_m$-equivariant morphism $\hat{f}: \hat{K}^\bullet \to \hat{L}^\bullet$ algebraizes uniquely to a $\mathbb{G}_m$-equivariant $f: K^\bullet \to L^\bullet$. Furthermore, if $\hat{f}$ is a quasi-isomorphism, so is $f$. In particular $f\vert_{t = 1}: K^\bullet\vert_{t = 1} \to L^\bullet\vert_{t = 1}$ is a quasi-isomorphism.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Notice that a $\mathbb{G}_m$-equivariant map is just a degree 0 map between graded free modules here. For two graded free summands $\mathbb{C}[[t]](w)$ and $\mathbb{C}[[t]](l)$, a degree 0 map $\mathbb{C}[[t]](w) \to \mathbb{C}[[t]](l)$ is equivalent to multiplication by a homogeneous element in $\mathbb{C}[[t]]_{l - w}$. The same is true for $\mathbb{C}[t]$, and notice that $\mathbb{C}[t]_{m} = \mathbb{C}[[t]]_{m}$ for all $m \in \mathbb{Z}$, so completion induces an isomorphisms of degree 0 parts
  \[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[t]}^\bullet(K^\bullet, L^\bullet)_0 \xrightarrow{\simeq} \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[[t]], \mathrm{cont}}^\bullet(\hat{K}^\bullet, \hat{L}^\bullet)_0\]
  and taking $H^0$ gives the algebraization statement. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Now suppose that $\hat{f}$ is a quasi-isomorphism. Put $C^\bullet \coloneq \mathrm{Cone}(f)$, then $\hat{C}^\bullet = \mathrm{Cone}(\hat{f})$ is acyclic. The support of $H^j(C^\bullet)$, which is a graded $\mathbb{C}[t]$-module, is a closed $\mathbb{G}_m$-invariant subset of $\mathbb{A}^1_t$, so it's either 0 or $\mathbb{A}^1$ or $H^j(C^\bullet) = 0$. Since $\mathbb{C}[t]_{(t)} \to \mathbb{C}[[t]]$ is faithfully flat, and $H^j(C^\bullet)_{(t)} \otimes_{\mathbb{C}[t]_{(t)}} \mathbb{C}[[t]] \simeq H^j(\hat{C}^\bullet) = 0$, we get that $H^j(C^\bullet)_{(t)} = 0$ for all $j$. Thus 0 is not in the support of $H^j(C^\bullet)$, hence $H^j(C^\bullet) = 0$ for all $j$. We get that $C^\bullet$ has to be acyclic; equivalently, $f$ is a quasi-isomorphism.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- prop-10: body_md -->
$\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$ has nilpotent residue.
<!-- end prop-10 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Since $\mathrm{gr}^{F_{\bar E}}\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E}) = \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}})$, every graded summand of $F_{\bar{E}}$ has nilpotent residues. $F_{\bar{E}}$ is a filtration by sub Higgs bundles, i.e., $\theta(F_i) \subset F_i \otimes \Omega^1_{\bar{X}}(\log D)$, so any residue $N_p$ around $p \in D$ must also preserve $F_{\bar{E}}$. Pick a basis at $p$ adapted to $F_{\bar{E}}$, then $N_p$ is a block upper triangular matrix, with diagonal blocks nilpotent, so $N_p$ must be nilpotent as well.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- prop-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:independence-framing-Rees-cochar}
  With the framing fixed, two different splittings of the socle filtration give the same framed bundle $\alpha_{\bar{E}}^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1)$. Changing the framing induces a change-of-framing action on $\alpha_{\bar{E}}^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1)$. After forgetting the framing, the resulting bundle is well defined up to a canonical isomorphism.
<!-- end prop-11 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Fix a framing $\phi: \bar{E}_x \to \mathbb{C}^r$. Let $s, s': \bigoplus_{j} \mathrm{gr}^S_j \bar{E}_x \xrightarrow{\simeq} \bar{E}_x$ be two different framings, and write $\bar{E}_x = s(\bigoplus_{j} \mathrm{gr}^S_j \bar{E}_x) \eqcolon \bigoplus_j G_j$. Put $\upsilon \coloneq s' \circ s^{-1}: \bar{E}_x \to \bar{E}_x$. With respect to a basis adapted to $\bar{E}_x = \bigoplus_j G_j$, write $\upsilon$ in block form $(\upsilon_{i,j})$ where $\upsilon_{i, j}: G_j \to G_i$. Notice that for $w \in G_j$, $s^{-1}(w) \in \mathrm{gr}_j^S \bar{E}_x$, thus $s'(w) \in S_j\bar{E}_x$, and $s'(w)\ \mathrm{mod}\ S_{j - 1}\bar{E}_x = w$ hence
  \[\upsilon_{i, j} = 0 \hspace*{\parindent} \text{for }i > j, \hspace*{\parindent} u_{i, i} = \mathrm{id}_{G_i}\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} Recall that for the splitting $s$, the Rees cocharacter $\gamma_s: \mathbb{G}_m \to \mathrm{GL}(\bar{E}_x)$ is $\gamma_s(t)\vert_{G_j} = t^{-j}\cdot \mathrm{id}_{G_j}$. Define $\varphi_{\upsilon}: \mathbb{G}_m \to \mathrm{GL}(\bar{E}_x)$ by $\varphi_{\upsilon}(t) \coloneq \gamma_s(t) \circ \upsilon \circ \gamma_s(t)^{-1}$. In a basis adapted to $\bar{E}_x = \bigoplus_j G_j$, we have the block form $(\varphi_{\upsilon}(t))_{i, j} = t^{j - i} \upsilon_{i, j}$. All exponents are nonnegative, $\varphi_{\upsilon}(0) = \mathrm{id}$, $\varphi_{\upsilon}(t) - \mathrm{id}$ is nilpotent, so by expanding $(\mathrm{id} + (\varphi_{\upsilon}(t) - \mathrm{id}))^{-1}$ we get a polynomial inverse $\varphi_{\upsilon}(t)^{-1}$. Define $g_{\upsilon}: \mathbb{G}_m \to \mathrm{GL}(\bar{E}_x)$ by $g_{\upsilon}(t) \coloneq \upsilon \circ \varphi_{\upsilon}(t)^{-1}$. Since $\gamma_{s'} = \upsilon \circ \gamma_s \circ \upsilon^{-1}$, we have 
  \[g_{\upsilon}(0) = \upsilon, \hspace*{\parindent} g_{\upsilon}(1) = \mathrm{id}, \hspace*{\parindent} g_{\upsilon}(t' \cdot t) \gamma_s(t') = \gamma_{s'}(t') g_{\upsilon}(t),
  \hspace*{\parindent} \alpha^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\bar{E}, s'}(t) = g_{\upsilon}(t) \cdot \alpha_{\bar{E}, s}^{\mathrm{DR}}(t)\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} For $A_n = \mathbb{C}[t]/(t^{n + 1})$, the change-of-framing of the $A_n$-point is $g_{\upsilon}(t)\ \mathrm{mod}\ t^{n + 1}$. Functoriality of \eqref{eq:formal-DR-Dol-immersion} (follows from the same diagrams in the proof of part (3)) gives that 
  \[\tau_{(E^{\mathrm{ss}}, \phi \circ s')} \circ \widehat{\alpha}_{\bar{E}, s'}^{\mathrm{DR}}(t) = g_{\upsilon}(t) \cdot (\tau_{(E^{\mathrm{ss}}, \phi \circ s)} \circ \widehat{\alpha}_{\bar{E}, s}^{\mathrm{DR}}(t))\] 
  which are both $\gamma_{s'}$-equivariant. By uniqueness of algebraization,
  \[\alpha_{\bar{E}, s'}^{\mathrm{Dol}}(t) = g_{\upsilon}(t) \cdot\alpha_{\bar{E}, s}^{\mathrm{Dol}}(t)\]
  and since $g_{\upsilon}(1) = 1$ we get the first statement. The change of framing is a matrix $T$; it is trivial to check that in each step of taking the Rees arc, formal isomorphism $\tau_{E^{\mathrm{ss}}}$, and algebraization, we are just conjugating by this fixed matrix. Hence $\alpha_{(\bar{E}, T \circ \phi)}^{\mathrm{Dol}}(t) = T \cdot \alpha_{(\bar{E}, \phi)}^{\mathrm{Dol}}(t)$. Forgetting the framing gives a canonical isomorphism (coming from that of framed bundles) of the underlying bundles at $t = 1$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:morphism-formal-transport-and-equiv-algebraization}
  $s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1) = H^0(\Phi^{\mathsf{SM}}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}})(f)$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Notice that over $\mathbb{A}^1_t$, $\mathrm{Rees}_S\bar{E} = (\alpha_{\bar{E}}^{\mathrm{DR}})^*\bar{E}^{\mathrm{univ}}$ (same for $\bar{F}$) so by base change for logarithmic De Rham complex, $\mathrm{Rees}_S f$ is an element of 
  \[\mathbb{H}^0(\mathbb{A}^1_t, \mathrm{L}(\alpha^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}})^* K_{\mathrm{DR}}^{\mathrm{univ}}) = \mathbb{H}^0(\mathbb{A}^1_t, P^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}})\]
  hence by \cref{lem:relative-hom-scheme-lemma}, is equivalent to a section $s^{\mathrm{DR}}_f: \mathbb{A}^1_t \to M^0_{\mathbb{A}^1_t}(P^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}})$ with $s_f^{\mathrm{DR}}(1) = f$; in fact $s_f^{\mathrm{DR}}(t)$ is exactly the morphism in the triple $\alpha_f^{\mathrm{DR}}(t)$. Applying $M^0(\Phi^{\alpha}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}})$ gives 
  \[s_f^{M}: \mathbb{A}^1_t \xrightarrow{M^0(\Phi^{\alpha}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}}) \circ s_f^{\mathrm{DR}}} M_{\mathbb{A}^1_t}(P^{\mathrm{Dol}}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}})\]
  \hspace*{\parindent} We have the following diagram, by definition of $\Psi_{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}, f^{\mathrm{ss}}}$,
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}, (\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}, f^{\mathrm{ss}})}} && {\widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}, f^{\mathrm{ss}})}} \\
	\\
	{M^0_{\widehat{R}_{\mathrm{DR}}^{\mathrm{pair}}}(K^\mathrm{univ}_{\mathrm{DR}})_{f^{\mathrm{ss}}}} && {M^0_{\widehat{R}_{\mathrm{Dol}}^{\mathrm{pair}}}(K^\mathrm{univ}_{\mathrm{Dol}})_{\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(f^{\mathrm{ss}})}}
	\arrow["{\Psi_{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}, f^{\mathrm{ss}}}}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["\simeq"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["\simeq", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{M^0(\Phi^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}})}", from=3-1, to=3-3]
  \end{tikzcd}\]
  and, by construction above, $M^0(\Phi^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\bar{E}^{\mathrm{ss}}, \bar{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}})$ pulls back to $M^0(\widehat{\Phi}^{\alpha}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}})$, so $\widehat{s}_f^{\mathrm{Dol}} = \widehat{s}_f^{M}$. Uniqueness of equivariant algebraization then gives $s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}} = s_f^M$. In particular, 
  \[s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1) = s_f^M(1) = H^0(\Phi^{\alpha}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}}\vert_{t = 1})(s_f^{\mathrm{DR}}(1)) = H^0(\Phi^{\mathsf{SM}}_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}})(f)\]
  as desired.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:functor-verification}
  $\mathsf{SM}: \mathsf{Conn}_{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X}, D) \to \mathsf{Higgs}_{\mathrm{nilp}}^{0, \mathrm{ss}}(\bar{X}, D)$ is a $\mathbb{C}$-linear functor.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
For this proof, we consider $\mathsf{SM}(f) = \alpha_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1)$, which is harmless by the previous lemma. For composition $\bar{E} \xrightarrow{f} \bar{E} \xrightarrow{g} \bar{G}$, we have $\mathrm{Rees}_S(g \circ f) = \mathrm{Rees}_S(g) \circ \mathrm{Rees}_S(f)$, and we have two different equivariant arcs $\alpha_{g \circ f}^{\mathrm{DR}}: \mathbb{A}^1 \to R^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}, g \circ f}$ and $(\alpha_f, \alpha_g): \mathbb{A}^1_t \to R^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}, f} \times R^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}, g}$. By \cref{thm:formal-isom-triples-and-composition}, we get a diagram, where $\mu_\Box$ are induced by compositions, 
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\mathrm{Spf}\ \mathbb{C}[[t]]} &&&& \\
	{} && {\widehat{Z}_{\mathrm{DR}, f, g}} && {\widehat{Z}_{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(f, g)}} \\
	{\mathrm{Spf}\ \mathbb{C}[[t]]} \\
	&& {\widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{DR}, \bar E, \bar G, g \circ f}} && {\widehat{R}^{\mathrm{trip}}_{\mathrm{Dol}, \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\bar E, \bar G, g \circ f)}}
	\arrow["{(\widehat{\alpha}^{\mathrm{DR}}_f, \widehat{\alpha}^{\mathrm{DR}}_g)}", from=1-1, to=2-3]
	\arrow[no head, from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow[shift left, no head, from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["{\Psi_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}, f} \times \Psi_{\bar{F}, \bar{G}, g}}", from=2-3, to=2-5]
	\arrow["{\mu_{\mathrm{DR}}}"', from=2-3, to=4-3]
	\arrow["{\mu_{\mathrm{Dol}}}", from=2-5, to=4-5]
	\arrow["{\widehat{\alpha}_{g \circ f}^{\mathrm{DR}}}", from=3-1, to=4-3]
	\arrow["{\Psi_{\bar{E}, \bar{G}, g \circ f}}", from=4-3, to=4-5]
  \end{tikzcd}\]
  so it follows that for the algebraized arcs, $\alpha^{\mathrm{Dol}}_{g \circ f}(1) = \alpha_g^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1) \circ \alpha_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1)$. Identity being preserved, as well as linearity, follows from the same argument using \cref{thm:formal-isom-triples-and-composition}.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- prop-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:fully-faithfulness}
  $\mathsf{SM}$ is fully faithful.
<!-- end prop-12 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Taking $H^0$ of \eqref{eq:quasi-isom-pulled-back-A1} we get
  \[H^0(\Phi_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}}^{\mathsf{SM}}): \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Conn}_{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X}, D)}(\bar{E}, \bar{F}) \xrightarrow{\simeq} \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Higgs}_{\mathrm{nilp}}^{0, \mathrm{ss}}(\bar{X}, D)}(\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E}), \mathsf{SM}(\bar{F}))\]
  and $\mathsf{SM}(f) = H^0(\Phi_{\bar{E}, \bar{F}}^{\mathsf{SM}})(f)$ so we are done.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- prop-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:exactness-and-socle-preservation}
  $\mathsf{SM}$ is exact and preserves the socle filtration. Moreover, for each $\bar{E} \in \mathsf{Conn}_{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X}, D)$, the filtration $F_{\bar{E}}$ on $\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$ coming from the algebraized Rees arc $\alpha_{\bar{E}}^{\mathrm{Dol}}$ is the socle filtration of $\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$.
<!-- end prop-13 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
$\mathsf{SM}$ is fully faithful, preserves length, and is essentially surjective on simple objects since the restriction to semisimple objects is $\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}$ which is an equivalence. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} Consider $0 \to \bar{E} \xrightarrow{i} \bar{F}$. Suppose for the sake of contradiction that $\ker \mathsf{SM}(i) \neq 0$. Since the categories are finite-length, there must be a nonzero simple subobject $0 \to A^{\mathrm{Dol}} \xrightarrow{u^{\mathrm{Dol}}} \ker \mathsf{SM}(i)$. By essential surjectivity on simple objects, $A^{\mathrm{Dol}} = \mathsf{SM}(A^{\mathrm{DR}})$, and fullness then lifts $A^{\mathrm{Dol}} \xrightarrow{u^{\mathrm{Dol}}} \mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$ to a nonzero morphism $A^{\mathrm{DR}} \xrightarrow{u^{\mathrm{DR}}} \bar{E}$. Next, 
  \[\mathsf{SM}(i \circ u^{\mathrm{DR}}) = u^{\mathrm{Dol}} \circ \mathsf{SM}(i) = 0\] 
  so by faithfulness, $i \circ u^{\mathrm{DR}} = 0$. But $\ker (i) = 0$ so $A^{\mathrm{DR}} = 0$, which is a contradiction. That $\mathsf{SM}$ preserves $\bar{E} \to \bar{F} \to 0$ follows from a dual argument. Thus $\mathsf{SM}$ is exact. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} For $\bar{E} \in \mathsf{Conn}_{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X}, D)$, $\mathsf{SM}(\mathrm{soc}(\bar{E}))$ is semisimple, hence lies in $\mathrm{soc}(\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E}))$. Conversely every simple subobject of $\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$ lifts to a simple subobject of $\bar{E}$, hence $\mathsf{SM}(\mathrm{soc}(\bar{E})) = \mathrm{soc}(\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E}))$. By exactness, we can induct to get that $\mathsf{SM}$ preserves the whole socle filtration. \\
  \hspace*{\parindent} For the last fact that $F_{\bar{E}} = S_\bullet$, consider the inclusion $f: S_i\bar{E} \hookrightarrow \bar{E}$. This induces $\mathrm{Rees}_Sf: \mathrm{Rees}_S(S_i\bar{E}) \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Rees}_S(\bar{E})$. By the proof of \cref{lem:morphism-formal-transport-and-equiv-algebraization}, we get a morphism over $\mathbb{A}^1_t$ by algebraizing the Rees section $\mathrm{Rees}_S f$, 
  \[s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}: \mathrm{Rees}_{F_{\bar{E}}}(\mathsf{SM}(S_i\bar{E})) \to \mathrm{Rees}_{F_{\bar{E}}}(\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E}))\]
  which is $\mathbb{G}_m$-equivariant and where $s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1) = \mathsf{SM}(f)$. Hence $s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(1)$ is injective by exactness. At $t = 0$, $s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(0) = \mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(f^{\mathrm{ss}})$, and the socle filtration of $S_i\bar{E}$ is a truncation of the socle filtration of $\bar{E}$, so $s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(0)$ is also injective. In fact we have a diagram 
  \[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\mathrm{gr}^F \mathsf{SM}(S_i\bar{E})} && {\mathrm{gr}^F \mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})} \\
	\\
	{\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\mathrm{gr}^S(S_i\bar{E}))} && {\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\mathrm{gr}^S\bar{E})}
	\arrow["{\mathrm{gr}^F \mathsf{SM}(f) = s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}(0)}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["\simeq"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["\simeq", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\mathsf{SM}^{\mathrm{ps}}(\mathrm{gr}^S f)}", from=3-1, to=3-3]
  \end{tikzcd}\]
  which implies that $\operatorname{im}(\mathrm{gr}^F \mathsf{SM}(f)) = \bigoplus_{j \leq i} \mathrm{gr}^F_j \mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$. $\mathbb{G}_m$-equivariance then implies that $s_f^{\mathrm{Dol}}$ is constant rank and injective over $\mathbb{A}^1_t$. Let $A \coloneq \operatorname{im}(\mathsf{SM}(f))$ then $A \subseteq F_i \mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$ since $\operatorname{im}(\mathrm{gr}^F \mathsf{SM}(f)) = \bigoplus_{j \leq i} \mathrm{gr}^F_j \mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$. Now, $A$ and $F_i\bar{E}$ have the same $\mathrm{gr}^F$, and same length hence they have the same ranks. Thus $A = F_i\bar{E}$. On the other hand, $\mathsf{SM}(f)$ the inclusion of $\mathsf{SM}(S_i \bar{E})$ into $\mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$, so $A = \mathsf{SM}(S_i \bar{E})$. Finally $F_i \bar{E} = \mathsf{SM}(S_i \bar{E}) = S_i \mathsf{SM}(\bar{E})$, as desired.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- prop-14: body_md -->
There are natural isomorphisms $\mathsf{SM} \circ \mathsf{Inv} \simeq \mathrm{id}$ and $\mathsf{Inv} \circ \mathsf{SM} \simeq \mathrm{id}$.
<!-- end prop-14 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
$\mathsf{SM}$ is a $\mathbb{C}$-linear exact equivalence of categories.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
By the previous proposition, $\mathsf{SM}$ is essentially surjective. It is also fully faithful and exact by \cref{lem:fully-faithfulness}, \cref{lem:exactness-and-socle-preservation}.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
